#2968. Energous: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Enterprise customers are moving past pilots toward 500-store rollouts, but whether that converts into recurring software revenue remains the open question for Energous.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 12, 2026, Energous (WATT -6.51%) announced that third-quarter revenue expanded to $3.4 million. Long-term investors need to see whether the business can convert initial trial programs into recurring software sales while restoring gross margin. Three things management said stand out.

Energous faced margin pressure earlier in 2026 due to manufacturing retooling and component costs. Price increases implemented in July lifted gross margin to 14% in the third quarter, and management targets historical hardware margins in the mid-30% range as offshore contract manufacturers that handle third-party assembly reach volume production.

Large enterprise customers in retail and e-commerce are moving past initial proof-of-concept testing, which evaluates whether the technology functions in real-world store environments. A major retail customer plans to expand coverage across 500 superstores and 150 warehouse club locations, which could accelerate Energous's transmitter hardware shipments.

Hardware sales provide initial adoption, but long-term enterprise value depends on software monetization. Energous is building out its e-Compass software platform to convert physical sensor readings into operational insights, establishing a foundation for recurring software-as-a-service subscriptions.

Revenue growth accelerated, and manufacturing operations stabilized across both contract assembly facilities. Energous held $28.8 million in cash at quarter end, providing runway to execute commercial rollouts without issuing equity. Management is tracking proof-of-concept conversions, customer expansion velocity, and gross margin progression toward its 40% long-term target.

Long-term expansion relies on embedding wireless power hardware as essential physical data infrastructure for enterprise automation.

#2966. Prediction: A Stock Market Crash Is Coming Eventually. Here's What Investors Should Do Based on What Happened in the Last 3 Crashes.

Even with ongoing selling pressure, the S&P 500 remains historically expensive.

Over the last few years, both the S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.89%) and Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC -1.28%) have benefited enormously from enthusiasm surrounding artificial intelligence (AI). History suggests a downturn is inevitable at some point, though.

While this doesn't mean a crash is coming tomorrow, one useful valuation metric suggests investors should prepare for the possibility of outsize volatility. Let's analyze the last three stock market crashes and see what important lessons we can uncover about what investors should do when stocks eventually plunge.

Developed by economist Robert Shiller, the cyclically adjusted price-to-earnings (CAPE) ratio compares the S&P 500's current price with its average inflation-adjusted earnings over the last 10 years. By smoothing out fluctuations in earnings per share (EPS), the CAPE ratio provides a longer-term perspective on stock market valuations.

Historically, the CAPE ratio has averaged approximately 18. But as of this writing (Nov. 13), it stands near 41. As the chart below illustrates, this places the stock market among its most expensive periods in history.

For perspective, the CAPE ratio reached 44 in December 1999, right before the dot-com bubble burst. Generally, prolonged readings between 30 and 40 have indicated elevated valuations and, consequently, increased vulnerability to economic shocks.

There is an important caveat to point out. The CAPE ratio does not predict when a stock market crash will occur. Stocks can remain expensive for a long time and valuations can normalize through earnings growth rather than falling share prices.

In addition, not every market crash starts with a rising CAPE ratio. The metric is more useful for evaluating long-term return expectations rather than accurately forecasting short-term market movements.

Nevertheless, today's elevated reading suggests investors should be mindful of the risks associated with paying historically high prices for stocks.

The last three major crashes reveal how painful market downturns can become.

The S&P 500 eclipsed its dot-com peak in May 2007, regained its pre-financial-crisis high in March 2013, and recovered its pre-pandemic record by August 2020. Despite their different causes, all three downturns eventually gave way to recoveries.

Let's consider what would have happened if an investor bought an S&P 500 index fund near the bottom of each downturn and held that investment through today.

These examples assume an investor purchased shares at the bottom, which is something that's nearly impossible to accomplish consistently. That's not the real takeaway, though.

The lesson here is that investors who held a diversified portfolio and remained invested through periods of uncertainty and volatility were ultimately rewarded by the stock market's resilience.

This is why buy-and-hold investing and a long-term outlook are the most effective strategies for navigating volatility. Investors can also use dollar-cost averaging to purchase stock regularly, reducing the temptation to time market bottoms.

Another stock market crash will eventually occur, although nobody knows when. History shows that investors who avoid emotional panic-selling and maintain a long-term time horizon will be better positioned to benefit from the inevitable recovery.

#2963. 1 No-Brainer AI Infrastructure Stock to Buy Hand Over Fist in November

One of this company’s businesses is very familiar to most of us.

Artificial intelligence (AI) infrastructure stocks are key players driving this technology revolution, and they have seen their shares soar over the past few years. But in recent quarters, the ride has been a bit bumpier.

Initially, investors were thrilled with these companies' revenue gains and recognized the potential of additional growth to come. But to support this growth, the infrastructure players had to invest heavily in data centers, and everything found within them, from chips to networking equipment. This meant hundreds of billions of dollars would be deployed -- from each player.

As this spending intensified, investors started to worry about the eventual revenue opportunity down the road, and that has prompted many to think twice about buying or holding onto infrastructure leaders. And that has weighed on the performance of some of these stocks.

With this in mind, let's check out one no-brainer AI infrastructure stock to buy hand over fist in November.

This company is a tech giant, known for something you use every day. And today, considering its track record of growth and long-term opportunities, it's looking pretty cheap. I'm talking about Alphabet (GOOG -2.17%) (GOOGL -2.13%).

You probably know the company very well for its Google platform, as most of us search for something on the internet and browse various websites on a daily basis. Google isn't the only search platform out there, but it has steadily held 90% market share over time. And it's even become a part of our vocabulary, with many of us saying "let's Google it," when looking for information.

All of this is great because it attracts advertisers in a big way, as they know they will find us, their target audience, across the Google platform. Advertising is Alphabet's biggest source of revenue, and it's growing, thanks to the company's development of AI. Alphabet is the name behind the Gemini family of models, and they are making Google Search better and the advertising experience more productive -- all of this is positive for revenue growth moving forward.

Now, let's get to the infrastructure story. Alphabet also has another major business, and that's Google Cloud. As one of the world's largest cloud service providers, it offers customers a broad range of AI and non-AI products and services. Here, demand is strong, particularly for AI tools and capacity. Alphabet has even started selling its in-house-developed chips, known as TPUs, to the data centers of select customers -- most of the revenue from this will be recognized next year, offering the company a potential new growth opportunity.

Google Cloud revenue has skyrocketed in recent quarters, and in the recent period it jumped more than 100% to $30 billion. With backlog of more than $560 billion and about half to be recognized as revenue in the coming 24 months, the next couple of years could be bright for the company.

That said, as mentioned above, investors have frowned on these hyperscalers' spending. Alphabet kept its capital spending guidance for the year unchanged in the recent quarterly report -- it expects to spend in the range of $195 billion to $205 billion.

While it's impossible to predict with 100% certainty the level of demand years down the road, it's reasonable to be confident about the moves of certain well-established companies like Alphabet. The tech giant has solid financials to support its investments and has visibility a couple of years out thanks to its backlog.

It's also important to note that we're in the early days of the actual use of AI in the real world. In recent years, AI has mainly involved the development and training of models; now we're rolling all of this out and looking to even more uses of AI down the road, such as in robotics. Meanwhile, trading at 22x forward earnings estimates, Alphabet looks particularly cheap, and all of this makes it a no-brainer buy in November.

#2962. The S&P 500 Is on Pace to Do Something That’s Only Happened 3 Times in History

Can the index finish strong and make it four times?

The S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.76%) tracks the largest 500 public U.S. companies, and it's the stock market's most popular index by a large margin. The first version of the S&P 500 was created in 1923, but it was later changed in 1957 to include 500 stocks, as we know it today.

Even tracing back to the earliest version of the S&P 500, it's on pace to accomplish something it has managed only three times in its 103-year history: four consecutive years of double-digit percentage returns.

There's a month and a half until year-end, so anything can happen, but as of now, the S&P 500 is up nearly 11% through Nov. 12.

Each of the previous three times the S&P 500 pulled off the four-year streak, massive macroeconomic shifts drove it. The first instance was during the World War II boom:

The second instance came a few years after World War II, as industrial activity ramped up and the U.S. was getting a post-war economic boom:

The third instance was during the dot-com bubble. As the internet became more mainstream, investors threw money at any "company" with a proper URL, driving valuations to unjustifiable and unsustainable levels. The S&P 500 pulled off five consecutive years of double-digit gains before the infamous bubble pop:

After a 2022 bear market, the S&P 500's impressive run has been fueled mainly by the current AI boom. It's been a tale of two markets, though. On one hand, many of the 500 companies in the index have produced mediocre returns.

On the other hand, much of the S&P 500's run has been driven by a handful of mega-cap tech stocks that make up a large share of the index and have seen their valuations explode over the past few years. From 2023 through the time of writing, the worst-performing "Magnificent Seven" stock has been Microsoft (MSFT -0.32%), which has more than doubled in value.

When over a third of the index (by weight) is performing that well, the overall index is bound to perform well. Granted, this year has been a bit different, with Apple, Amazon, and Microsoft being the only ones with positive gains, but their weight in the index has done some of the heavy lifting.

The stock market is unpredictable, especially in the short term. Just because the S&P 500 is up a double-digit percentage right now doesn't mean it will be in six weeks, when the year ends.

After a 4.4% single-day decline on Nov. 5 (its largest since April 2025), the S&P 500 was only up just above 7% for the year. Who's to say that won't happen again, especially with its largest holding, Nvidia (8.39% of the index), dropping 13% to open on Nov. 12 after its longtime CEO resigned. Unexpected events at companies with a large influence on the index can throw a curveball.

That said, I would lean more toward the S&P 500 continuing its streak versus finishing the year (hopefully) up in the single-digit percentage range. Eventually, the streak has to stop, as corrections and bear markets are a natural part of the stock market cycle, but who knows if that'll be in days, weeks, months, or years.

A couple of things working against the S&P 500 right now are stretched valuations and investors' mixed views on some of the major AI stocks (which, for better or worse, account for a lot of the S&P 500). Regardless, an S&P 500 ETF is an investment you can feel comfortable adding to regularly, regardless of the short-term noise or movements.

Past results don't guarantee future performance, but it has been one of the market's most resilient long-term investments.

For release at 4:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation and the Federal Reserve Board today published feedback letters for resolution plans submitted in October 2025. The agencies did not identify any shortcomings or deficiencies in these resolution plan submissions.

Resolution plans, also known as living wills, describe a banking organization's strategy for orderly resolution in the event of material financial distress or failure. The agencies conducted a joint review of the 2025 resolution plan submissions from 15 banking organizations with over $250 billion in assets.

In addition, the agencies determined that the shortcoming previously identified in the 2021 BNP Paribas resolution plan has been satisfactorily addressed.

#2958. Federal Reserve Board finalizes changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 9:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Wednesday finalized changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements. The two final rules are largely similar to the proposed rules from 2025. The Board also requested comment on a proposal to better capture differences in banks' business models to generate fee income.

The Board conducts stress tests to ensure that large banks are sufficiently capitalized and able to lend to households and businesses even in a severe recession. In December 2024, the Board announced it would modify the test to improve its resilience.

"The stress test is an essential component of our regulatory capital framework," said Vice Chair for Supervision Michelle W. Bowman. "Today's changes preserve its resilience by ensuring that it is transparent, granular, and risk-sensitive. The public will now have greater assurance that the risks banks take will be reflected appropriately in their stress test losses and their capital requirements."

The first final rule requires the Board to invite public input annually on the stress test scenarios and any material model changes. It also updates the framework that guides the design of the hypothetical scenarios and adopts the models that will be used for the 2027 stress test. In addition, the final rule makes several adjustments to the stress test calendar. The final rule also updates the global market shock component, which is a set of shocks to specific market components applied to banks with large trading books. These banks will now be tested against two global market shock components each year, and the Board will use the shock that produces the largest losses for each firm to calculate the firm's stress test results.

The second final rule requires the Board, when calculating stress capital buffer requirements, to average the results from the two most recent annual supervisory stress tests for firms subject to the stress test in both years. The Board will begin averaging stress capital buffer requirements in 2028 to ensure that only models incorporating public input are used in the calculation.

The proposal would request public comment on a revision to the Board's noninterest income model that would improve the stress test's ability to capture business model diversity across firms. If adopted, these adjustments would replace the current noninterest income model that projects each bank's fee income under stress. Comments are due 60 days after publication in the Federal Register.

Together, the changes are likely to reduce year-over-year volatility in capital requirements by approximately 50 percent and are not expected to materially affect aggregate capital requirements.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

Federal Register notice: Modifications to the Capital Plan Rule and Stress Capital Buffer Requirement

#2953. Tare: “Senza il Covid penso avremmo vinto quello Scudetto. Lotito e Inzaghi…”

Ai microfoni del podcast Sky Calcio Unplugged condotto da Gianluca Di Marzio è stato intervistato l’ex diesse della Lazio, Igli Tare

Ai microfoni del podcast Sky Calcio Unplugged condotto da Gianluca Di Marzio è stato intervistato l’ex diesse della Lazio, Igli Tare.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Sono felice per la Lazio di oggi. La società ha creato una squadra competitiva e ha scelto Gattuso, che ha creato un clima positivo: tutti gli vogliono bene. Questa è una società con grande storia, rimarrò sempre legato alla Lazio. Ho un legame forte con questo club.

Abbiamo avuto degli approcci ma era più un'idea di Lotito. Conosco Rino, ho un rapporto molto bello con lui. Sono molto contento per lui.

Penso di sì. Era una storia molto bella. Prima della pandemia eravamo devastanti, dopo 20' eravamo già 2-0 o 3-0 per noi". Una chiacchiera significativa con Inzaghi, al tempo allenatore della Lazio: "All'inizio della stagione parlai con Simone e mi disse: ‘Igli, sei preoccupato?’. E io: ‘Io per niente, sono straconvinto che lotteremo per vincere lo Scudetto’. E lui: ‘Ma tu sei matto’. E io: ‘Fidati che sarà un'annata particolare ma molto bella per noi.

Penso che Ciro sia un ragazzo unico. Molto sensibile, aveva bisogno dell'ambiente che gli dava fiducia. E poi è stato straordinario, ha scritto la storia della Lazio. Un giocatore irripetibile.

Parliamo di un centroavanti fenomenale per il modo di interpretare il gioco e nella Lazio, per vari motivi, non ha dato quello che poteva dare. Aveva un aereo privato con destinazione Parigi al PSG. Ma lui ha rifiutato per venire alla Lazio. Ha rifiutato anche più del doppio di stipendio che gli è stato proposto da altre parti per venire in Italia.

Lui aveva l'ambizione di giocare il Mondiale 2014 e all’epoca nessun club gli dava un contratto con più di un anno. C'erano Galatasaray, Valencia, ma sempre per un anno. Io gli ho dato un contratto di tre anni, ho convinto anche Lotito. Quando abbiamo firmato tutti mi hanno massacrato perché stavamo pagando uno di 32 anni che aveva vissuto un anno negativo con qualche infortunio. Ma dopo il primo/secondo allenamento tutti hanno capito che era un fuoriclasse.

Con lui ho un legame molto speciale, abbiamo giocato insieme. Ha intrapreso una carriera che per tanti era una sorpresa ma per me mai. La reputo una persona molto curiosa, intelligente nella gestione. Poi è cresciuto tanto con l’esperienza alla Lazio e all'Inter. Quando tornerà, non so se in Italia o all’estero, penso che sarà un allenatore completo. L'esperienza più bella che ho vissuto? Con Delio Rossi. Ero giocatore con lui per tre annie quando Lotito gli comunicò che io sarei stato il nuovo DS lui rimase sorpreso e disse 'allora mi vuoi mandare via?’. Cambiare da giocatore a diventare "il capo di un allenatore" era una sorpresa anche per me, non mi aspettavo questa scelta di Lotito.

#2952. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 stocks for the long haul

Global stock markets have been volatile as investors assess high bond yields and continued tensions between the U.S. and Iran. There are concerns about the impact of uncertainty in the Middle East on oil prices and consequently on inflation.

Investors who prefer to look beyond short-term noise and buy stocks with attractive long-term growth potential can follow the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts. The ratings of these experts are backed by in-depth research of a company's financials and thorough analysis of the factors affecting its business performance.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Semiconductor giant Nvidia (NVDA) reported stellar fiscal second-quarter results and reassured investors about continued strength in AI-driven demand for its chips. Moreover, Nvidia's FY28 revenue growth outlook of 70% was well above the Street's expectations.

Reacting to the Q2 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reiterated a buy rating on NVDA and increased his price target to $300 from $288. "This is our Top Pick in the semis group, with a compelling product cycle, exceptional growth and valuation below peers," said the 5-star analyst.

Nvidia delivered impressive quarterly results despite supply constraints, Moore said, but he believes that the FY28 revenue and gross margin commentary mattered more, with a favorable outlook for both measures.

Moore said Nvidia's 70% revenue growth guidance for FY28 was above his own estimate of 52% and the consensus of about 40%. The top-line growth estimate was a "remarkable figure" given supply issues and Moore said he expects Nvidia to continue to address bottlenecks to deliver rapid growth.

Among other takeaways, the analyst said performance metrics for Vera Rubin chips were strong, with Nvidia emphasizing a 30x higher throughput per megawatt and 35x lower token cost compared with Grace Blackwell Ultra.

Moore ranks No. 159 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 60% of the time, delivering an average return of 24.60%. See Nvidia Statistics on TipRanks.

Ride-hailing and delivery platform Uber Technologies (UBER) was recommended by BMO Capital analyst Brian Pitz, who discussed the company's opportunity in the autonomous vehicle (AV) market in a recent research note that reiterated a buy rating and price target of $119.

The 5-star analyst highlighted Uber's transition into a diversified marketplace, with investors now focusing on its AV growth prospects. The key question is how much value can Uber generate as AVs become commercialized, Pitz asked.

"We continue to believe that Uber's AV strategy is evolving and will become a significant driver of revenue and profitability," the analyst said.

Importantly, Pitz believes that Uber can establish itself as a preferred mobility platform for AV makers, supported by its growing AV infrastructure capabilities and an expanding partner base. This would enable Uber to win a significant share of value as the AV market is commercialized.

Additionally, Pitz noted Uber's enhanced position in the AV value chain, with the company shifting from serving as a distribution channel for robotaxis to becoming a broader platform for AV mobility. The analyst discussed Uber's efforts to capture growth opportunities in the AV market, including higher capital spending, expansion into new markets and building partnerships beyond Alphabet's Waymo.

Pitz ranks No. 574 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of about 12.20%. See Uber Technologies Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Semiconductor company Marvell Technology (MRVL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the second quarter of FY27. But the company's increased FY28 guidance fell short of investors' high expectations.

In reaction to the Q2 FY27 results, KeyBanc analyst John Vinh reaffirmed a buy rating on Marvell Technology, with a price target of $400. The analyst noted that Marvell's results and outlook were driven by strength in its data center business. The Q2 FY27 data center business grew 46% year-over-year, to $2.17 billion, far above KeyBanc's estimate of $2.09 billion.

Management "highlighted accelerating AI networking demand across scale-out, scale-across and scale-up architectures, supported by strong 800G optical DSP demand, rapid 1.6T ramp, broader 51.2T switch deployments and higher TIA/driver demand," Vinh said.

Consequently, Marvell raised its data center revenue guidance, now expecting 60% growth in FY27 and more than 60% in FY28.

Regarding the recently announced $120 billion warrant arrangement with Google, Vinh said management argued that programs tied to the warrant are already priced into the custom outlook through FY28. Meanwhile, new programs and those in execution are expected to drive significant upside to the previous FY29 custom XPU revenue estimate of over $10 billion, Vinh said, citing management.

Vinh ranks No. 123 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 60% of the time, delivering an average return of 28.50%. See Marvell Technology Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2938. Top Wall Street analysts recommend these 3 dividend stocks for higher returns

Dividend-paying stocks can enhance portfolio returns by providing a steady income stream to investors and the potential for capital appreciation. Several investors seek exposure to dividend stocks in a volatile backdrop to ensure stable income.

In this regard, recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, backed by strong financials and cash flows to support consistent dividend payments.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Energy Transfer (ET) is a diversified midstream energy company with about 140,000 miles of pipelines and associated energy infrastructure in 44 states. The company made a quarterly cash distribution of 34 cents per common unit for the second quarter of 2026. At an annualized payment of $1.36 per common unit, ET offers a yield of 6.3%.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Jeremy Tonet reiterated a buy rating on Energy Transfer stock and slightly raised his price target to $25 from $24. The analyst noted that the company delivered a strong Q2 beat across all key metrics.

Tonet highlighted that Energy Transfer raised its 2026 adjusted EBITDA guidance to $18.8 billion-$19.1 billion from $18.2 billion-$18.6 billion, reflecting robust base results and cost optimization. The 5-star analyst added that the company "tightened" its capex to $5.6 billion–$5.9 billion and expects capex above $5 billion annually through 2029, given solid opportunities.

About the Hugh Brinson pipeline, Tonet noted Phase 1 (1.5 bcfd) reaching full capacity by September 1 and Phase II capacity (0.7 bcfd) going online by Q1 2027. The analyst also noted completion of a 14-mile Hugh Brinson lateral in Abilene, Texas, and final stages of talks for an additional 250 mmcfd of Oklahoma power plant demand.

"All in, we see ET well positioned to continue capitalizing on organic growth opportunities across the value chain," said Tonet.

Tonet ranks No. 922 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 9%. See Energy Transfer Financials on TipRanks.

This week's second dividend pick is independent oil and natural gas company Permian Resources (PR). The company declared a base dividend of $0.16 per share for the third quarter, payable on September 30, 2026. At an annualized dividend of $0.64 per share, PR offers a yield of about 2.7%.

Recently, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Permian Resources stock and increased his price target to $27 from $22, citing an improved outlook on oil production growth and operational efficiencies.

The 5-star analyst noted that while PR stock has outperformed its peers so far in 2026, he sees further upside. Mehta is confident about the company's operational improvements and ability to deliver incremental free cash flow per share through the strategic ground game program. He added that the program has completed about $1.05 billion in bolt-on deals this year (as of August 5).

"We see the continued success of PR's ground game as constructive toward future inventory replenishment and increasing working interest over time," said Mehta.

The analyst also emphasized Permian Resources' solid track record of generating growth in free cash flow (FCF) per share. Mehta expects the company's free cash flow per share to grow at a 20% CAGR (compound annual growth rate) from 2025–2028.

Mehta ranks No. 401 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.8%. See Permian Resources Statistics on TipRanks.

Moving on to Sempra Energy (SRE), a utility holding company. Earlier this month, the company declared a quarterly dividend of $0.6575 per share, payable on October 15. At an annualized dividend of $2.63 per share, SRE stock offers a yield of about 3.1%.

Recently, Jefferies analyst Julien Dumoulin-Smith upgraded SRE stock to Buy from Hold but slightly lowered the price target to $97 from $101. The 5-star analyst noted that Sempra stock is trading at a price/earnings multiple that is 14% below its electric peers due to concerns about Texas transmission capital expenditure and the failure of California legislation.

Smith noted that the derating of weaker California peers PG&E (PCG) and Edison International (EIX) is weighing on the sum-of-the-parts (SOTP) valuation but is becoming less relevant given their materially higher fire risk compared to Sempra Energy.

Furthermore, Smith expects Sempra Energy's Texas capex plan to stay in place despite the opposition to transmission and data centers, though delays to 765-kV projects are likely. While a full recovery for SRE stock will need a clearer outcome from the Texas legislative session, Smith believes that the stock may already be an early opportunity for patient investors.

"We are early on the upgrade but see limited downside at current levels as the market is pricing in little transmission upside," said Smith.

Smith ranks No. 945 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 8.2%. See Sempra ETF Exposure on TipRanks.

#2930. Federal Reserve Board releases results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

LAZIO: Motta; Leite, Doekhi, Rovella, Pinamonti, Cancellieri, Pedraza, Przyborek, Lazzari, Cataldi, Gudmundsson.

A disp.: Renzetti, Pannozzo, Pellegrini, Noslin, Floriani M., R. Bordon, Serra, Farcomeni, F. Bordon, Morelli, Gatto, Santagostino, Montano, Sulejmani.

MGARR UNITED: Chetcuti; Borg, Buhagiar, D'Aprile, Farrugia, Gatt, Igiebor, Job, Pace Cocks, Sant, Maduka.

A disp.: Agius, Basay, Bindia, Frendo, Magro, Mifsud, Muscat, Sammut, K. Vella, N. Vella, Yepez Morlets.

Fuori: Rovella, Motta, Cancellieri, Lazzari, Doekhi

Dentro: Farcomeni, Renzetti, Montano, Floriani, F. Bordon

Pinamonti ad un passo dal gol. L'attaccante della Lazio ha provato a indirizzare la palla con il destro, ma la conclusione termina di poco a lato

Sugli sviluppi di una punizione, Sant tutto solo svetta in area, ma colpisce male e la palla termina fuori

Przyborek sulla sinistra serve Rovella al limite dell'area che va alla conclusione e trova il gol complice un Chemcuti che prova a bloccare la sfera non riuscendoci e buttandosi la palla in rete.

Sbaglia Cataldi in impostazione che riparte in contropiede guadagnandosi un corner con la conclusione di Ighiebor deviata

La Lazio va a riposo avanti 2-0 grazie ai gol di Cancellieri e Diogo Leite

Pinamonti svetta tutto solo in area sfruttando un bel cross dalla destra. La conclusione però termina alta sopra la traversa

Alla terza occasione personale Diogo Leite trova il gol. Bel corner calciato da Gudmundsson, che trova l'incornata vincente di Diogo Leite sul secondo palo dopo che la traiettoria era stata sporcata da una deviazione sul primo palo.

Gudmundsson serve Przyborek che va al cross rasoterra, velo di Pinamonti, arriva Diogo Leite che da buona posizione spara alto.

Bella triangolazione tra Pinamonti e Cataldi che trovano il pertugio giusto per servire Cancellieri, che a tu per tu col portiere non sbaglia e trova il gol.

Recupero di Pinamonti al limite dell'area, che va subito alla conclusione ancora una volta, però, troppo centrale

Si libera sulla destra Manuel Lazzari, ma il cross con il mancino attraversa tutta l'area di rigore senza trovare i compagni

Conclusione sul primo palo di Igiebor, che impegna Motta, sul tap-in si avventa Buhagiar, che travolge però il portiere biancoceleste reattivo sulla ribattuta

Colpo di testa di Diogo Leite sul calcio d'angolo battuto da Gudmundsson, palla che termina di poco a lato

Spalle alla porta Pinamonti si gira e va alla conclusione, traiettoria troppo centrale: blocca Chetcuti

Timida ripartenza della formazione maltese guidata da Job, che prova il cross dentro, ma non c'è nessuno che segue l'azione

Gudmundsson dal limite si gira ed esplode il mancini, devia in corner Chetchuti

Recupero alto della squadra di Gattuso, Taylor prova la conclusione ma si oppone Chetcuti

Squadre ancora nello spogliatoio, leggero ritardo nella tabella di marcia

#2924. Luka Dončić scores 43, leading Lakers over Suns for eighth consecutive win, tying for NBA's top record

Luka Dončić continued his scorching start to the 2026-27 NBA season, topping the 40-point mark for the seventh time in 12 games in the Los Angeles Lakers' 131-123 win over the Phoenix Suns on Sunday. He currently leads the NBA in scoring at 38.6 points per game.

Dončić scored 20 of his 43 points in the first quarter, hitting three 3-pointers as he shot 5-for-10 and the Lakers took a 33-24 lead. That advantage increased to 54-37 with five minutes to go before halftime on a Jake LaRavia 3. Yet the Suns closed out the second quarter on a 12-3 run to trim the Lakers' lead to 61-54.

In the third quarter, the Lakers started hot from behind the arc, hitting three 3-pointers (two by Quentin Grimes) as part of a 13-3 run and boosted their lead to 20 points. Eventually, that margin became 31 points with under two minutes remaining in the quarter. Dončić added 12 points to put him at 37 points going into the final 12 minutes.

Phoenix recovered to give the Lakers a game in the fourth quarter, hitting four 3-point attempts to begin the frame. A Walker Kessler tip-in dunk restored the Lakers' advantage to 113-92 with just under nine minutes remaining.

However, the Suns responded with a 15-1 surge, fueled by six points from rookie Koa Peat. A 3 from Ryan Dunn cut the Lakers' lead to 117-111 at the 4:40 mark. Collin Gillespie drove to the basket for a layup that pulled Phoenix to within five points with 1:05 left in regulation.

But another 3 from Grimes effectively put the game away for the Lakers and Dončić closed out the scoring with two free throws. However, he told sideline reporter Roqui Theus afterward that the plan was for him to sit out the fourth quarter until the Suns made the game close.

"I wasn't planning on playing the fourth quarter," Dončić said. "We gotta get better at that. … Once we get up, we gotta stay there."

The Lakers played without Austin Reaves, who missed the game due to a bone bruise in his left heel.

Overall, Dončić shot 13-of-28 from the floor, while making 12-of-14 free throws. He also notched nine assists in his 43 minutes of play. Grimes followed with 22 points, shooting 5-for-6 on 3-pointers, with 6 assists and 2 steals. Collin Sexton contributed 16 points off the bench and Kessler added 14 points with 12 rebounds.

For the Suns, Gillespie finished with 26 points (shooting 5-for-10 on 3s), 4 assists and 4 rebounds. Luke Kennard added 18 as the Suns got 78 points from their reserves. Devin Booker led the Phoenix starters with 17 points and 7 assists.

The Lakers have won eight consecutive games and improved their record to 12-2, tying the San Antonio Spurs for the NBA's best record. (The Spurs defeated the Utah Jazz on Sunday, 110-102.) Their next game is Tuesday versus the Charlotte Hornets (4-9).

#2922. Top Wall Street analysts find these 3 stocks attractive as long-term investments

Investors have been grappling with stock market volatility, weighing elevated oil prices, persistent geopolitical tensions, the Federal Reserve's rate hike and a debate about AI safety. For all that, it helps to look beyond short-term noise and focus on stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors gain clarity, as these experts conduct in-depth research to pick stocks with attractive growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Enterprise software and cloud infrastructure company Oracle (ORCL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the first quarter of fiscal 2027. The quarter benefited from solid growth in cloud infrastructure revenue.

After hosting a series of investor meetings with Ken Bond, Oracle head of investor relations following Q1 earnings, Guggenheim analyst John DiFucci reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $400. Oracle remains a Guggenheim Best Idea, he said.

"We continue to believe ORCL is a decade stock, driven by the massive (and profitable) opportunity ahead for AI training and inferencing," said DiFucci. He also cited the traditional Public Cloud business and additional AI-enabled cloud database and cloud applications for his bullish stance. DiFucci expects all the factors to drive revenue and profit acceleration over the next few years.

While Oracle delivered 850 MW of data center capacity in Q1 FY27, Bond cautioned that Infrastructure as a Service revenue in any quarter depends more on the timing of when that capacity comes online, than on the headline MW number. DiFucci expects IaaS growth to stay strong, with full-year growth expected above Q1 FY27's 120% constant currency rate. Management expects total revenue growth to accelerate sequentially throughout fiscal 2027, as the faster-growing infrastructure business makes up a larger share of the mix.

DiFucci addressed concerns regarding customer concentration, saying OpenAI's share of total remaining performance obligations has dropped from 80% last June to about 50% now, and is expected to keep falling as new contracts are signed with new and existing customers.

DiFucci ranks No. 263 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 62% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17.5%. See Oracle Insider Trading Activity on TipRanks.

Space systems and launch services provider Rocket Lab (RKLB) was recently initiated as a buy with an $80 price target by Raymond James analyst Brian Gesuale.

"RKLB has assembled launch, spacecraft, components, payloads and optical communications, with Neutron and Iridium completing medium-lift and applications/spectrum in 2027," said Gesuale.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that with the completion of the Iridium acquisition, Rocket Lab will become the only vertically integrated space company with positive free cash flow (FCF). The deal will add a deployed constellation valued at more than $3 billion, globally coordinated L-band spectrum and about $500 million in earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) and $300 million in free cash flow (FCF).

Gesuale highlighted RKLB's strong revenue and backlog growth and progress toward positive EBITDA and FCF in 2027/2028. Rocket Lab is moving from a heavy investment phase into monetization, with estimated revenue growth of 59% in 2026, the analyst said, noting that RKLB's backlog has more than doubled to almost $2.4 billion from $1.1 billion in Q3 2025.

Given strong growth projections, the analyst expects RKLB's gross margins to expand from about a 35% trough in 2027 to about 47% by 2030, while R&D spending is estimated to decline from ~35% of sales in 2026 to about 10% in 2028, creating substantial operating leverage as the platform matures.

Gesuale ranks No. 369 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17%. See Rocket Lab Statistics on TipRanks.

J.P. Morgan analyst Doug Anmuth recently upgraded Meta Platforms (META) to buy from hold and raised his price target to $820 from $640. Despite the year-to-date underperformance of META, the 5-star analyst still sees significant upside, as Meta is in the early stages of launching frontier models and AI-powered offerings beyond advertising, particularly the Muse AI agent and Meta Model API access.

"Frontier models are at the core of Meta's product and monetization pipeline over a multi-year period, and its path toward superintelligence," said Anmuth.

In the summer of 2025, Meta set a goal to rebuild its Meta Superintelligence Labs team and deliver frontier AI models within a year, the analyst said. The team appears to have met its goal, rapidly advancing from the rollout of Muse Spark 1.1 in July to Muse Spark 1.3, which is competitive with Claude and GPT models, Anmuth said.

Meanwhile, Meta's Watermelon product will expand opportunities in consumer products, business intelligence, and Family of Apps engagement, the analyst said. The product will enhance internal operations and overall efficiency, said Anmuth, who's confident of Meta's ability to bring consumer-driven AI products to its extensive user base of 4 billion and views its distribution network as a competitive advantage.

While Meta has focused on frontier models and AI product development, Anmuth also sees solid opportunity in its AI infrastructure capacity, which he believes will support future compute needs.

Anmuth ranks No. 832 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of about 9.40%. See Meta Platforms Financials on TipRanks.

#2918. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for steady income

Stock markets remain volatile as investors weigh uncertainty in the Middle East, rising interest rates and concerns about the artificial intelligence (AI) trade.

Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking a stable income stream can enhance their portfolios with the addition of dividend stocks. In this regard, tracking recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks that are backed by strong financials.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is the first dividend pick. Earlier this month, the company paid a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share. At an annualized dividend of $7.12 per share, CVX offers a dividend yield of about 3.5%.

After a meeting with senior leadership, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on CVX and raised his price target to $240 from $225. Among the key takeaways Mehta emphasized was that Chevron is building a solid pipeline of promising international exploration projects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa and the Eastern Mediterranean. In particular, he mentioned the focus on Venezuela, where Chevron expects to more than double gross production to 600 kbd from about 280 kbd by 2031, using three joint ventures.

Mehta also noted Chevron's efforts to deploy new technologies, especially in shale and tight-oil operations. CVX is focused on boosting productivity and efficiency to maximize free cash flow, he added.

Initiatives such as artificial lift optimization, AI and machine learning, and advanced chemical treatments are expected to improve recovery by about 10% in new wells. Consequently, capital spending per barrel in U.S. shale is projected to fall 25% in 2026, with total Permian spending dropping below $3.5 billion.

Mehta also said that Chevron sees power as a growth opportunity and is using its U.S. natural gas portfolio, key equipment and partnerships to meet rising demand. Notably, Chevron recently signed a 20-year power purchase deal with Microsoft (MSFT) to supply 2.67 GW of behind-the-meter capacity. This project, called Kilby, is expected to see its first power delivery in 2028 and generate mid-teens returns and long-term cash flows.

Mehta ranks No. 536 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 11.3%. See Chevron Statistics on TipRanks.

Enterprise Products Partners (EPD) is a master limited partnership (MLP) that provides midstream energy services to producers and consumers of natural gas, natural gas liquids (NGLs), crude oil, refined products and petrochemicals. With a quarterly cash distribution of 56 cents per common unit ($2.24 per common unit annualized), EPD offers a yield of about 6%.

Following a recent call with the company, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on EPD with a price target of $42. The analyst slightly lowered her estimates for the second half of 2026, citing margin and volume normalization, though demand dynamics remain strong.

While the 5-star analyst expects a sequential decline due to normal seasonality and normalization following high spreads in the first half of the year, she still expects EPD to report another solid quarter (Q3 2026) and enter 2027 with a favorable setup. Ahead of Q3 earnings, Scotto expects investors to focus on export demand durability, EPD's ability to profit from spot cargo sales and growth in Permian volumes.

Scotto continues to expect $150 million of common unit buybacks per quarter in the second half of 2026. She expects buybacks to increase to $200 million per quarter in 2027, as capex spending declines and free cash flow improves.

In total, Scotto is bullish on EPD and views it as a core MLP holding that has both offensive and defensive characteristics. The analyst praised EPD's diverse asset base as ensuring stable cash flows. "Furthermore, the partnership's multi-year organic growth backlog helps provide visibility on long-term distribution growth," Scotto said.

Scotto ranks No. 199 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 15.9%. See Enterprise Products Partners Financials on TipRanks.

Brookfield Infrastructure Partners (BIP) operates a diversified portfolio of high-quality utilities, transport, midstream and data assets. The company declared a quarterly distribution of 45.5 cents per unit, payable on September 29. At an annualized distribution of $1.82, BIP offers a yield of 5.2%.

Recently, BMO Capital analyst Devin Dodge reaffirmed a buy rating on Brookfield Infrastructure with a price target of $47. The analyst cited improved visibility into BIP's growth and views its corporate simplification as a potential catalyst.

"Layering in the benefits from the proposed corporate simplification, a multiple well below historical levels and an attractive yield, we believe BIP offers a compelling risk/reward and it remains a preferred idea," said Dodge.

With more organic growth projects in the pipeline and new partnership investment opportunities, Dodge believes that BIP has clearer visibility into where it will deploy capital. That should support continued double-digit growth in funds from operations (FFO) per unit.

Dodge said the semiconductor foundries that BIP is developing with Intel (INTC) are expected to be fully commissioned by the end of 2026, with returns improving through Q4 2026. Dodge expects the joint venture to boost FFO by 300-400 basis points.

Dodge ranks No. 476 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 68% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.5%. See Brookfield Infrastructure Partners KPIs on TipRanks.

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2916. Game Recap: Furious comeback by Suns bench falls short in 131-123 loss

The Lakers came out of the gates hot, and the Suns did not. From there, every time the Suns started to put together a run, the Lakers answered with a bigger run. And while the bench gave the Suns some hope with a furious comeback attempt in the fourth quarter, the Suns dug themselves far too big a hole to climb out of, ultimately falling to the Lakers at home, 131-123.

If the Suns had any hope of an upset, they needed to slow down the Lakers' potent offense, and they failed to do that for much of the afternoon. The Lakers came out of the gates hot and did not really slow down. Luka scored 20 in the first quarter on the way to a 43-point afternoon (together with nine assists), but the Suns' inability to stop Lakers not named Luka, inability to keep the Lakers off the offensive boards, and inability to stop the three is what ultimately did the team in.

Quentin Grimes scored 22 points for the Lakers, including 11 in the third quarter, while shooting 5-of-6 from three. The Lakers grabbed 12 offensive rebounds – with Walker Kessler having six of them – which led to 21 second-chance points. And the Lakers shot 16-of-41 (39%) from three, with Luka and Grimes combining to hit 10 of them.

For the Suns, the story was the fact that the starters dug such a deep hole that the bench could not get them out. Devin Booker was 4-14, Jalen Green was 4-11, and Dillon Brooks was 0-of-2 (what?!). The Suns actually out-rebounded the Lakers (42 to 40) and grabbed more offensive rebounds than the Lakers (14 to 12), but not thanks to the starters. The bench grabbed 29 of the Suns' 42 rebounds and 12 of their 14 offensive rebounds. But the Suns' bench not only outperformed the starters on the boards, but they also outperformed them overall.

Collin Gillespie led the Suns in scoring with 26 points (including 5-of-10 from three). Jordan Goodwin led the team in rebounds, with seven. Ryan Dunn grabbed four offensive rebounds, and Luke Kennard chipped in another three.

And let's not forget Koa Peat. Mr. Arizona sparked a furious fourth-quarter rally that saw the Suns' bench cut a 24-point Lakers lead to just five, before running out of steam down the stretch.

Overall, this game ended up being much closer than it felt throughout the afternoon. The Suns started the fourth quarter down by 24, and the bench was entirely responsible for the team nearly pulling this one out. Notably, Jordan Ott stuck with the bench throughout that quarter. He did not go back to the starters. It felt like the right move, but also truly confirms the lack of cohesiveness with the starting five.

At the end of the day, the Suns miss out on a chance to get to .500, falling to 5-7, as the Lakers win their eighth in a row to move to 12-2.

The Suns got off to the slowest start we've seen since opening night, letting the Lakers score the first 10 points of the game — eight of them by Luka — before Miles Bridges scored the team's first points with 9:24 left in the first quarter. The Lakers followed that 10-2 start by going on a 13-3 run, which left the Suns trailing by 18 after just over 5 minutes of playing time.

Jordan Ott went to the bench, and the bench and Devin Booker managed to trim that lead to 9 by the end of the quarter. A Booker jumper, a Collin Gillespie three, then a Kahman Maluach layup on a nice feed from Book sparked a 7-0 run, to make it a 23-12 game. From there, Book and Maluach were responsible for the rest of the Suns' points that quarter – with Book scoring eight and Man Man chipping in four.

Maluach flashed some of his defensive potential on a play where he challenged and altered a layup attempt by Sandro Mamukelashvili and showed a quick second jump by immediately blocking a Jarred Vanderbilt layup attempt.

At the end of the first, the Suns found themselves down 33-24.

With Luka out to start the second, the Suns needed to take advantage of the non-Luka minutes – and, at first, it looked like they did. The Suns started the quarter with a Gillespie three to cut the lead to six, but that was the closest they would get (until the fourth). The Suns ended up losing the non-Luka minutes – letting Collin Sexton score eight points in the second. By the time Luka came back in the game with 4:56 left in the half, the Suns found themselves down 17.

That lead ballooned to 19, before the Suns started chipping away again. At the 3:36 mark, they were down 19. At that time, the story of the game was the difference from three (with the Lakers being +12) and from the free-throw line (+7). The Lakers also grabbed seven offensive rebounds in the second frame alone.

The only player showing any true effort on the boards for the Suns at this juncture was Jordan Goodwin — who was fouled on two separate occasions while trying to grab offensive rebounds off missed free throws.

Despite only having 10 points from Booker in the first half, the Suns were only down 10 at halftime.

The third quarter started a lot like the first. The Lakers were scoring and we could not. After the Lakers scored the first eight points, the Suns finally got some points from Devin Booker with 9:28 left. But it continued to be all Lakers from there.

Just like Collin Sexton stepped up for the Lakers in the second quarter, the third quarter belonged to Quentin Grimes. Grimes, who came in shooting 28% from three on the season, scored 11 in the quarter, including three 3-pointers. Luka also scored in double figures in the third quarter, chipping in 12.

In the third, the Suns shot 9-of-21 overall and 2-of-11 from three, while the Lakers shot 14-of-23 overall and 5-of-9 from three.

The fourth quarter started with little hope and a 24-point deficit. The Suns came out and promptly scored the first six points, and after the Lakers got the lead back to 22, Collin Gillespie hit back-to-back threes to cut the lead to 16 with 10 minutes left.

But then the offensive rebounds started coming back. Thybulle had a tip-in off a missed three, then on the next possession, Kessler had a put-back dunk on a missed layup by Thybulle. The Suns were down 19 with 8:15 left, and Koa Peat checked in – making it look like Jordan Ott had decided to pack it in.

But Peat checking in resulted in a furious comeback attempt that made a mundane afternoon into an exciting one.

Peat immediately impacted the game, fighting off Kessler on a Suns miss, which allowed the team to keep possession, then scoring on a strong shot over Kessler.

Collin Gillespie was also not ready for the game to be called, as he drew an and-1 on the next possession, to get the Suns within 14. This brought Luka and his 37 points back into the game. The first possession of Luka's return resulted in a turnover, and Gillespie missed a step-through jumper, but Peat was there for the tip-in. Suns down 12 with just over six minutes to go.

The Suns used a 10-1 run to find themselves down single digits with 5:02 to go. And they kept chipping away.

A Ryan Dunn three cut the lead to six with 4:37 left. Jake Laravia answers with a three of his own, but then Luke Kennard drops one to make it a six-point game again, but a missed three by Laravia followed by an offensive rebound and then a BANKED in three by Lavaria sealed the game for the Lakers.

An exciting comeback, but, ultimately, a 131-123 loss — and a loss that just further magnifies the obvious disconnect amongst the Suns starters. But still, Koa Peat…

The Suns hit the road immediately for an away matchup tomorrow against the Denver Nuggets.

#2909. 3 bleak thoughts after the Mavericks’ no-show in 131-94 loss at the Boston Celtics

The last three games make it clear. This season is all about managing expectations for fans of the Dallas Mavericks (7-7). But good grief.

The Mavericks dropped the second game of a back-to-back set in horrifying fashion, 131-94, in a Sunday matinee against the Boston Celtics (11-2) at TD Garden.

The unbridled optimism that came along with a surprise 7-4 start has been tempered, to say the least, after a pair of stinkers at the Minnesota Timberwolves and the Cleveland Cavaliers, followed by Sunday's scheduled loss, when the Mavs were again without star 34-year-old guard Kyrie Irving, due to injury management (rest).

The Celtics poured in three of their first four 3-point attempts to jump out to an 11-2 lead three minutes into the game, and the tone was set quickly. The Mavs didn't make their first shot from the field until Zaccharie Risacher hit a long jumper from the corner with 5:22 left in the first. The starting lineup of Cooper Flagg, Caleb Martin, Naji Marshall, Daniel Gafford and PJ Washington provided zero floor spacing on the offensive end.

And so, naturally, the Celtics' early lead swelled to 19-4 after Jordan Walsh went to the basket for six straight points on three straight possessions less than five minutes into the game. If Cooper Flagg isn't going out of his mind on offense, the roster as constructed for Sunday's game just doesn't stand a chance against the league's best teams.

Boston canned seven triples in the first quarter, while the Mavs shot an abysmal 2-of-18 from the field. The game was basically over as the Celtics led by an appalling 42-9 count after one. It goes without saying that the nine points were the lowest scoring output for the Mavs of the young 2026-27 season, but what was worse is that the team appeared to let it affect their effort on defense, as well.

The Mavericks woke up a little in the second quarter, but waking up after you're down 35 points is not worth a whole hell of a lot. A little 9-0 run late in the frame pulled the Mavs to within 59-34, but it seems laughable to even mention developments like that in the course of such a monumental shellacking.

Dallas turned the ball over 12 times in the first half to offset the team's five second-quarter 3-point makes, and Boston carried a lopsided 67-37 lead into the halftime break.

Flagg's early run of brilliance to start the season has hit a hard car crash of a stop. He shot a combined 11-of-31 in the two losses leading up to Sunday's game at Boston, and he followed that up by missing all three of his first-quarter shot attempts.

We see apex Flagg, as we've learned, when he plays next to competent guards, and that was simply not the case against the Celtics. The starting lineup at Boston featured exactly zero guards, and when Walter Clayton Jr. and Marcus Sasser entered halfway into the first, they offered little to no help on either end. Clayton came back in the second and scored eight points on two made 3-pointers, but the damage had been done at that point.

The Celtics' defense concentrated all their efforts into containing Flagg, because Flagg was the only viable source of offense left on Dallas' injury-riddled roster. He finally connected on his first field goal early in the second, on the low block through the foul of Payton Pritchard. But after two more 3-balls from the Celtics on the other end, the Celtics' lead had ballooned to 50-15 with nine minutes left in the first half.

There will be angst, and a certain amount of angst is warranted here, but circumstances aren't helping the 19-year-old star, either. He scored just five points in the first half and finished with 17 on 5-of-15 shooting in the loss.

Let's just get this out of the way and move on, shall we? Jayson Tatum torched the Mavericks for 21 of his game-high 32 points in the third quarter to lead way for the Celtics in the win. Derrick White added 16. Eight different Celtics made two or more 3-pointers, including Tatum, who put in five of them.

Risacher led the Mavericks with 18 off the bench on 8-of-9 shooting.

#2907. Top Wall Street analysts are upbeat about the prospects for these 3 stocks

September ended on a mixed note, with the S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average falling 0.5% and 4.3%, respectively, while the Nasdaq Composite climbed 1.9%. Markets remained on edge with higher oil prices and Treasury yields weighing on investor sentiment and triggering fears of additional interest rate hikes.

In this climate, investors with a long-term horizon can track recommendations from top Wall Street analysts to find stocks with solid growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Neocloud company CoreWeave (CRWV) is benefiting from robust AI-driven demand for cloud computing.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Samik Chatterjee upgraded CoreWeave to buy from hold, raising his price target to $125 from $120. The analyst cited favorable pricing, driven by solid demand, for the upgrade.

Chatterjee said CoreWeave is now more willing to sign premium priced, shorter-term contracts, which lift his revenue and margin estimates. CRWV stock performance has been underwhelming as investors worry about high capital spending, the analyst noted. That said, he believes that higher prices and increased margins will more than offset the rise in debt to fund capital spending, improving the stock's valuation.

Chatterjee noted the 25% price hike in July across CoreWeave's product offerings and frequent price increases by rivals like Nebius (NBIS), adding that some peers are charging nearly three times more for short-term compute contracts than what CoreWeave charges on longer-term deals.

"CoreWeave's leverage of the opportunity [is] evident in its recent press release highlighting contracts signed in F3Q at ~$40M/MW," said Chatterjee. JPMorgan said higher pricing is already contributing 5-10 percentage points to contract margins versus earlier deals.

Chatterjee ranks No. 4 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 75% of the time, delivering an average return of 55.3%. See CoreWeave Statistics on TipRanks.

Palo Alto Networks (PANW) is a provider of cybersecurity solutions across network, cloud, security operations, AI and identity. Following a discussion with management, BTIG analyst Gray Powell reiterated a buy rating on PANW and raised his price target to $425 from $404.

After a review of Palo Alto's product-level growth catalysts, Powell sees clear upside to both revenue and next-generation security (NGS) annual recurring revenue (ARR) in fiscal 2027. He expects PANW to deliver pro forma revenue growth of more than 17%, above the consensus estimate of 14.6%, and NGS ARR growth of more than 26% versus the Street's expectation of 24.5%.

Over the past five years, the Palo Alto business mix, has moved to higher-growth product areas and new markets. from what was earlier viewed as a hardware-centric network security company, Powell said. The 5-star analyst estimates that hardware firewalls and attached subscriptions now account for less than 50% of revenue.

Meanwhile, high-growth solutions, including Prisma SASE, software firewalls, Cortex XSIAM, Chronosphere and Idira now represent more than 35% of PANW's revenue. "Investors may not fully realize the growth potential within PANW's platform story," Powell said.

BTIG is optimistic about the prospects of Chronosphere in observability, CyberArk in identity and software firewalls and Prisma AIRS in the network and AI security business.

Powell ranks No. 320 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 17.3%. See Palo Alto Networks KPIs on TipRanks.

In a research note on September 30, Rosenblatt analyst Scott Devitt reiterated a buy rating on e-commerce and cloud computing platform Amazon (AMZN), raising his price target to $360 from $335. Fears that agentic commerce poses a major threat to Amazon's advertising business seem "overstated," he said.

Devitt noted that the growing adoption of personal agents and AI shopping assistants has sparked concern about displacement of Amazon's retail media business. But the 5-star analyst remains bullish, given Amazon's ability to adapt to changing consumer behavior, as it's done in the past.

Additionally, Devitt argues that even if an AI agent helps a shopper discover a product, Amazon can still handle the purchase and delivery, keeping much of the transaction value. He highlighted that management believes that existing Amazon shoppers will likely continue to start their shopping journey on the platform because it has better personalization, accurate pricing and inventory information that third-party agents still lack.

Overall, Devitt expects Amazon's retail business to prove a net beneficiary of agentic commerce. Fewer clicks would not hurt the company if the value of each remaining click and overall volumes keep rising, he said. The argument is that even if Amazon loses some sponsored ad revenue over the next few years, it would likely be only a small part of the company's total business and could be offset by growth in other areas.

"Consistent with our AI framework, we continue to view Amazon as one of the most resilient leaders in our coverage, combining owned compute infrastructure with a physical fulfillment moat," said Devitt. His bullish view is supported, Devitt said, by expectations that Amazon Web Services (AWS) will exit the year at a more than 45% growth rate with continued margin improvement.

Devitt ranks No. 765 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 49% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Amazon Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

#2903. Celtics throttle Mavericks, 131-94, thanks to magnificent 42-9 1st quarter

Some NBA games are won late in the fourth quarter, with dramatic twists and turns in the final seconds and elite shotmaking from the game's brightest stars.

Sunday was not one of those games. This one was over before many fans settled into their seats, as the Celtics led by 33 through 1 en route to a dominant 131-94 victory at TD Garden.

"We were playing well, forcing them into shots we want them to take, and they missed some shots," said Celtics coach Joe Mazzulla.

Boston (11-2) has now won six straight, including four by 20 or more, and is still in first place in the Eastern Conference. Dallas (7-7), playing without Kyrie Irving (knee) on the second night of a back to back, looked outmatched in every facet early.

Jayson Tatum led the way with 32 points (11-of-18 shooting), 6 rebounds and 6 assists. Boston shot 51 percent as a team, leaving no doubt and improving to 6-0 at home on the season.

The Celtics absolutely dominated the first quarter, building a 42-9 advantage through 1 with a truly spectacular performance. Yes, it was the largest lead after the first quarter in franchise history. It felt like it, too.

Boston shot 59 percent from the floor and 54 percent from 3, while Dallas was at 11 percent from the field and 0 percent from distance. The Celtics racked up 14 points in the paint, compared to 2 for the Mavericks, and won the rebounding battle, 16-10.

Boston could do no wrong. Dallas could do no right.

"We tried to shrink the floor as much as possible and cause havoc," said Celtics wing Jordan Walsh.

Walsh played with conviction and purpose and helped set the tone early, and Derrick White (16 points) found a rhythm early to help extend the lead. Seemingly every shot the Celtics threw up went in, as the action resembled a varsity team scrimmaging a junior varsity team.

The Mavericks found their footing in the second quarter and chipped away, but the Celtics preserved a 67-37 lead heading into halftime following a relatively even second quarter.

"Obviously it's easy to really bring it when it's a close game, or things like that," Tatum said. "Just trying to challenge our mindset of we're up 25 to 30, can you try to put the game away? I think we did a really good job of that today."

Tatum, Pritchard and White continued to flourish, and Sam Hauser provided strong minutes off the bench. Zaccharie Risacher (18 points) paced Dallas with 9 in the half, but Cooper Flagg (17) struggled and the Mavericks turned it over six more times in the quarter.

Eleven players saw the floor for Boston and nine scored, led by White with 11.

The Celtics kept their target practice going in the third quarter, as Paul George and Walsh hit back-to-back corner 3's to push the margin to 34. Neemias Queta added one of his own from the other corner to improve to 4 of 5 from distance on the season.

"His growth and his role has gone kind of under the radar," Mazzulla said of Queta. "He's more important than just with defense. He's important in the locker room, he's important on the offensive end. He continues to get better and better. It's easy to overlook it because of how easy he makes it."

Flagg got going in the third, but Tatum continued to cook and kept the lead hovering around 30 most of the way.

Tatum drilled a step-back 3 with 4.3 seconds left to make it 104-71 through three. He finished the quarter with 21 and continued to look like pre-injury Tatum.

The Celtics started the fourth with Baylor Scheierman, Devin Carter, Ron Harper Jr., Hugo González and Luka Garza, and they continued to play the brand of basketball that got Boston to that point.

Harper Jr. converted inside, Scheierman and Harper Jr. swished consecutive 3's and Garza delivered inside to push it to 40, 117-77, with 8:58 to go.

"The start of the fourth quarter, we made them call the first timeout," Mazzulla said.

Gino Time came with 2:51 remaining and the Celtics up 36. The win streak is alive, and the Celtics are looking very much like contenders.
