#2857. Heating oil prices are up 59% from last winter. The spike is 'crushing folks,' Maine nonprofit says

Winter heating bills can strain household budgets. This year, a spike in heating oil prices could make the season especially difficult for households that depend on the fuel and have little financial wiggle room.

The national average price of heating oil was roughly $5.91 a gallon as of Nov. 9, up about $2.19, or 59%, from a year earlier, according to the U.S. Energy Information Administration.

In Maine, the average price per gallon is roughly $5.83, up about $2.49 from a year earlier, or about 75%. The state is among those whose residents may feel the price rise acutely — about 50% of homes there use heating oil.

The jump "is crushing folks," said Jason Parent, CEO of AVEC Community Action, a nonprofit agency based in Presque Isle, Maine, that provides heating assistance and other social services in three of the state's 16 counties. Many residents face immediate crises, he said, such as empty fuel tanks or reliance on unsafe heating sources.

"My fear is that there are households out there who have secondary fuel sources like wood stoves, pellet stoves and other things that are no longer operating and functioning in a safe way," Parent said.

The price surge comes on the heels of years of high overall inflation that has already squeezed household budgets, he said.

"The cost of living has made it impossible for households who had been figuring out how to get by without [financial help]," Parent said.

The price spike comes amid a tight global market for refined fuels, a category that includes heating oil and diesel. Supply shocks tied to the Iran war and the Russia-Ukraine war, low inventories and limited refining capacity have constrained supplies and added upward pressure on fuel prices, according to recent research from the Federal Reserve Bank of Kansas City.

The EIA predicts that a warmer winter in the Northeast will reduce consumption, helping to offset higher prices. In October, the agency forecast that the average household using heating oil will spend $2,115 from November through March, about 21% more than last year.

Meanwhile, homes primarily heated with natural gas are expected to spend an average of $640 this winter, down 9%, while those using propane are forecast to spend an average of $1,246, down 3%, according to the EIA's October forecast. Households using electricity for heating are expected to spend an average of $1,196, up 4% from a year ago. These are national figures.

Roughly 4.1 million U.S. households use heating oil as their primary means of heating their homes, according to the EIA. Most of them — close to 3.4 million — are located in the Northeast.

Unlike electricity or natural gas, heating oil is delivered in large quantities. Payment terms vary, but some dealers require a minimum delivery or charge extra for smaller orders. In Maine, for example, ordering at least 100 gallons for a standard 275-gallon tank can avoid a surcharge, according to the state attorney general's office. At current prices, that 100 gallons would cost about $600 — a tall order for some households.

AVEC's Home Energy Assistance Program, or HEAP, provides financial assistance to qualifying households, based primarily on income and the number of people living in the home. Funding originates with the federal Low Income Home Energy Assistance Program, or LIHEAP, which is run by the U.S. Health and Human Services Department and overseen by state agencies, often working with local organizations such as AVEC.

Maine and tribal grantees in the state received about $38 million from LIHEAP in October, according to a press release from outgoing Republican Sen. Susan Collins. AVEC administers about $4 million of that through HEAP, Parent said. AVEC also gets donations from community fundraising to help residents cover heating costs.

A component of HEAP is the Energy Crisis Intervention Program, which serves qualifying families who face an energy crisis, such as a potential electric shutoff or an empty oil tank and no financial means to replenish the supply.

AVEC began taking calls for that program on Nov. 2, Parent said.

"In the first hour alone, there were 150 voicemail messages on our emergency fuel line," Parent said. "People were in crisis before the program opened up."

Catherine Lafleche of Hersey, Maine, describes AVEC as a lifeline.

The 65-year-old is guardian to two grandchildren, ages 11 and 6, and is living mostly on about $1,100 in monthly Social Security benefits and state guardianship assistance, after shattering her upper-arm bone in a fall in April. Her injury required reverse shoulder replacement surgery, from which she continues to recover. Before that fall, she had worked as a substitute teacher and bookkeeper.

"I'm $6,000 short of income at this point in the year," Lafleche said.

Hersey is in Maine's northernmost county, Aroostook, where January and February bring average high temperatures in the 20s and average lows in the single digits, based on National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration data from 1991 to 2020 recorded at a Caribou Municipal Airport weather station. Last winter, the area experienced severe cold snaps, including on Jan. 24, when the high was minus 2 degrees Fahrenheit.

Lafleche applied to HEAP through AVEC in August, but a backlog meant potentially waiting until November for assistance. Unable to afford more fuel when it became chilly, she ran out of heating oil in early October and relied on a small wood stove to keep part of her house warm, burning Amazon boxes collected from other local residents because she didn't have enough firewood.

In late October, an AVEC representative called to say a charitable donation would cover 100 gallons of heating oil.

"When the oil man came, I cried," Lafleche said.

For residents in Maine who need help covering the cost to heat their homes and are seeking out help, "the need is outpacing the benefits," said Parent, of AVEC.

The average benefit through HEAP is about $750 per household this season, up from roughly $550 a year ago, Parent said. "That $750 is going less far than the $550 did last season" for heating oil, he said. "It's buying fewer gallons because of the precipitous increase."

The benefits are generally paid directly to the fuel supplier as a credit on the household's account. Even if a household qualifies, funding may run out.

"We're in a world of hurt now ... but it's going to be crisis time again in January or February," Parent said.

The Trump administration, in an effort to bring down heating oil prices, said it would release 300,000 barrels of heating oil from the Northeast Home Heating Oil Reserve, according to a Nov. 4 release from the U.S. Energy Department.

Whether adding this extra heating oil to the market will reduce prices for residents depends at least partly on how it gets doled out, said Jonathan Rubin, director of the Margaret Chase Smith Policy Center and a professor of economics at the University of Maine.

"No one really knows the economic impact, because will the home-heating oil distributors release it in one month, which would make it about 10% to 12% of a single month's supply? Or will they release it over the entire season, in which case it's only about one and a half percent of the entire season's home heating oil supply?" Rubin said.

"I would say that this is a modest short-term buffer," he said.

Congressional lawmakers have called for increased LIHEAP funding this year due to rising energy costs, including heating oil. Dozens of House lawmakers — mostly Democrats — signed an Oct. 14 letter to the House Appropriations Committee seeking an additional $3 billion in emergency supplemental funding for LIHEAP.

That followed an Oct. 1 letter from a group of Democratic senators and Sen. Bernie Sanders, I-Vt., to Robert F. Kennedy Jr., secretary of the Health and Human Services, also asking that he seek at least $3 billion more for LIHEAP.

Already, the government released roughly $3.63 billion in LIHEAP funding nationwide on Oct. 9 — 90% of the roughly $4 billion available under a continuing resolution that is currently funding federal agencies.

The timing for disbursing the remainder depends on when Congress either passes full-year funding for the 2027 fiscal year — which started Oct. 1 and runs through Sept. 30, 2027 — or passes another stopgap measure to keep the government running. If lawmakers do neither by Dec. 11, a federal shutdown would begin.

Whether the additional $3 billion requested for LIHEAP will be considered in any upcoming congressional appropriations process is uncertain.

Lafleche, the Hersey resident, said the 100 gallons of heating oil donated to her, along with some firewood she has now purchased, should keep her home warm until early December. She hopes to receive approval for benefits by then through HEAP, which has helped her in past years.

"We're not in this position by choice. Most of us made every plan to the contrary, but it stacked up to be not enough. And I feel like we don't deserve to be cold," she said.

#2855. Recap: Rockets rout Wizards, 147-119

The Washington Wizards fell to the Houston Rockets, 147-119, on Sunday night at Capital One Arena, extending their losing streak to seven games. The Wizards trailed by as many as 37 points and were it-rebounded by 19.

Coming off a 30-point performance against the Detroit Pistons on Thursday, Alex Sarr scored eight quick points on 3-of-3 shooting, including a three-pointer, to give Washington an early 13-12 lead.

The Wizards closed the first quarter trailing 36-31, with Sarr leading the way with 10 points. Anthony Davis, who also scored 30 against Detroit on Thursday, had a much quieter start, missing all three of his field-goal attempts, including several looks around the rim.

Houston opened the second quarter with a 7-0 run and eventually stretched its lead to 12. Sarr briefly halted the momentum with another three-pointer, but the Rockets continued to pull away. Houston's lead reached 17 with 6:29 remaining in the half, as Amen Thompson and Kevin Durant helped fuel the offensive surge. Thompson started 3-for-3 from beyond the arc, while Houston shot 56% from three-point range in the first half.

Washington went into the break trailing 73-54 despite Sarr's 15 points, four rebounds and two assists. The rest of the Wizards' starting lineup — Trae Young, AJ Dybantsa, Kyshawn George and Davis — combined for just 18 points.

Houston picked up right where it left off to start the third quarter, opening the period with a 12-4 run, and the game wasn't competitive from that point forward.

Thompson (26 points), Durant (25), Tari Eason (25) and Alperen Sengun (21) all scored 20+, while Sarr (25) was the only Wizard to finish with more than 20 points. Dybantsa scored 16 and grabbed seven boards, Davis finished with 14 points and eight rebounds, and George tacked on 11 points and four assists. Will Riley knocked down two three pointers and scored 14 points off the bench.

The Wizards will turn around to host the Memphis Grizzlies on Monday night at 7:00 p.m. ET at Capital One Arena.

#2853. Lutnick faces questions over Nvidia spy scandal. His sons' firm has ties to the chipmaker

In late September, Cantor Fitzgerald hosted Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang and other top executives from the chipmaker for investor meetings in New York, according to a Cantor research report reviewed by CNBC.

The Wall Street firm liked what it heard.

"We continue to view NVDA as the best risk/reward in our coverage," Cantor analyst C.J. Muse and his colleagues wrote in a research report dated Oct. 3 that maintained a $350 price target and overweight rating on the stock.

Yet the firm, formerly run by Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick and now led by his sons, Brandon and Kyle, reported a nearly 50% decline in the number of Nvidia shares it held between September 2025 and March 2026, according to a CNBC analysis of securities filings that excludes options. Nvidia had been one of the largest stock positions reported in Cantor's filings.

The decline coincided with White House and U.S. intelligence officials pressing Lutnick to crack down on Megaspeed, a Singapore-based company suspected of funneling Nvidia's advanced artificial intelligence chips to China. Lutnick repeatedly resisted those efforts, Axios reported Nov. 11.

By the time Cantor hosted Huang in New York, Lutnick had also received a classified intelligence warning, according to a Nov. 10 Axios report, about Huang's ties to a suspected Chinese spy and had appeared alongside the CEO at a G20 technology summit in North Carolina.

Even as Cantor pared back its Nvidia stake, it continued doing business across the chipmaker's AI empire. A CNBC review of securities filings, corporate announcements and Wall Street research found the firm has helped arrange billions of dollars in financing for Nvidia's business partners while maintaining close ties to the chipmaker's leadership.

Those ties have drawn fresh scrutiny following Huang's resignation Thursday and Axios' reporting that Lutnick repeatedly resisted a crackdown on companies suspected of smuggling Nvidia chips to China, despite President Donald Trump authorizing new export restrictions on those companies for a third time on Nov. 5.

An ethics expert said the relationship between Lutnick and his former firm's investments may raise questions, given what the secretary knew about Nvidia.

Richard Painter, the former chief White House ethics lawyer for President George W. Bush and now a corporate law professor at the University of Minnesota, said that while "there's no proof of" Lutnick sharing nonpublic information with Cantor Fitzgerald, "the appearance doesn't look great."

"His conversations with his sons better be about publicly available information," Painter told CNBC.

There is no evidence that Lutnick's sons had advance knowledge of the intelligence concerns, that changes in Cantor's reported holdings were tied to any warnings or that the firm's business interests influenced their father's decisions.

"Cantor has to date from time to time had only immaterial stand-alone proprietary positions in Nvidia, unlike the positions reflected in 13F filings which were entered into in the ordinary course of business in connection with client swap transactions, that are common in institutional markets," a Cantor spokesperson told CNBC.

Cantor declined to answer CNBC's other questions. The Commerce Department, the White House, Howard Lutnick, Brandon Lutnick and Kyle Lutnick did not respond to CNBC's requests for comment or detailed questions about the findings.

As scrutiny of Huang intensified, Cantor Chairman Brandon Lutnick was publicly praising the chipmaker's leadership. "Nvidia is one of the great companies on the planet, and they have an amazing leadership team," Brandon Lutnick told Fox Business on Wednesday.

Trump is standing by Howard Lutnick. A White House official told Reuters on Thursday that the president maintains "complete confidence" in the commerce secretary.

But cracks in the administration's unity emerged Friday, when Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent told CNBC's "Squawk Box" that the departments of Treasury, Defense and State and the Office of the U.S. Trade Representative all "were in favor of putting Megaspeed on the entity list for months."

Bessent pointed to Commerce as the department responsible for imposing the entity list, which identifies restricted businesses and other groups, adding, "You will have to ask them about their internal processes."

Nvidia was Cantor's second-largest reported stock position before Howard Lutnick left Wall Street to become commerce secretary in February 2025, behind only bitcoin investor MicroStrategy, now known as Strategy. At the end of 2024, the firm disclosed more than 3.5 million Nvidia shares worth $479 million, according to its quarterly securities filing.

That share count held steady through September 2025, but its value climbed 36% to $652 million, according to Cantor's quarterly securities filing, as the Trump administration moved over the summer to ease restrictions on Nvidia's H20 chip sales to China.

Over the following six months, however, the number of Nvidia shares Cantor reported holding fell by 49%, to 3 million in December and 1.78 million by the end of March 2026, with their value dropping from $652 million to $310 million, according to a CNBC analysis of securities filings.

Pressure on Howard Lutnick was also mounting on Capitol Hill. Sens. Jim Banks, R-Ind., and Elizabeth Warren, D-Mass., in March urged the secretary to suspend or reconsider Nvidia's export licenses after three people linked to Nvidia customer Super Micro Computer were indicted on charges of conspiring to smuggle AI chips to China.

"The Trump administration, I think, leaks like a sieve when it comes to nonpublic government information getting to various hedge fund guys," Painter said, though he stressed there is no evidence Howard Lutnick had shared such information with his sons.

The filings do not establish why Cantor's reported share count fell or whether its overall financial exposure to Nvidia declined. Nvidia’s stock had more than doubled from its April 2025 lows by late October, before retreating in the following months. The stock fell about 6.5% between the end of September and the end of March.

Cantor's reported Nvidia holdings remained near that lower level through June 30, the latest quarter covered by public disclosures, at 1.81 million shares. Its next quarterly filing is due Monday.

Cantor has not publicly explained the decline.

Most of those shares were attributed to CF Secured, a Cantor brokerage subsidiary that uses stocks and derivatives to hedge customer transactions, according to its audited financial statements. Cantor also disclosed substantial Nvidia options positions during the time period CNBC examined, overwhelmingly skewed toward put options, which can protect against declines in the stock price.

As concerns about Huang escalated inside the Trump administration in May, according to the Nov. 11 Axios report, Cantor was helping finance the expansion of one of Nvidia's newest business partners, securities filings show.

On May 7, Nvidia and Australia-based cloud computing provider IREN announced a five-year, $3.4 billion cloud computing contract and a strategic partnership giving the chipmaker the right to invest up to $2.1 billion in IREN.

"That's not a passive financial investment. Nvidia is a partner who wins as we deliver," IREN co-CEO Daniel Roberts told investors on the company's quarterly earnings call that day.

Five days later, as Trump headed to China aboard Air Force One, Secretary of State Marco Rubio warned him about Huang's relationship with a suspected Chinese spy, The New York Times reported Tuesday. Huang, who had not initially been invited on the trip, joined the president's delegation during a refueling stop in Alaska.

Howard Lutnick had already been sent a highly classified intelligence memo detailing concerns about Huang's relationship, according to the Nov. 10 Axios report.

On May 12, the day Trump departed for China, IREN announced the pricing of a convertible bond offering that ultimately raised $3 billion when it closed two days later. Cantor was one of seven bookrunners on the deal, alongside JPMorgan Chase and Goldman Sachs.

On the day the offering closed, May 14, Lutnick's Commerce Department quietly cleared roughly 10 Chinese companies, including Alibaba and ByteDance, to buy Nvidia's advanced H200 chips, though some of those approvals predated the intelligence memo.

It is unclear when IREN hired Cantor for the offering or how much the firm earned from the deal. Cantor declined to answer CNBC's questions about its work with IREN.

Cantor reported a substantial position in IREN's stock.

By the end of June, Cantor reported $115 million in IREN shares and $362 million in Nvidia shares. Together, the two companies accounted for more than 9% of the value of Cantor's reported $5.2 billion in securities holdings, excluding options, according to CNBC's analysis of the filing.

IREN was not the only company in Nvidia's orbit that turned to Cantor for financing.

In October 2025, Cantor advised AI infrastructure developer Lancium on a $600 million financing deal to support its data center campus in Abilene, Texas, the first site of Stargate, the massive AI infrastructure project backed by OpenAI, SoftBank and Oracle.

Newmark, a commercial real estate firm controlled by Cantor, also helped finance the Abilene campus, arranging a $7.1 billion construction loan in May 2025.

Ten months later, Nvidia agreed to invest $2 billion for a roughly 20% stake in Lancium, with up to $1 billion more tied to future milestones, The Information reported in August. Lancium, which is also backed by Blackstone, confirmed an investment and a technology partnership on Aug. 24 without disclosing financial terms.

It's unclear whether Cantor continued advising Lancium after the financing deal or played a role in Nvidia's investment. Neither Cantor nor Lancium responded to questions about the investment.

Cantor's reported ties also extend to Nvidia's supply chain.

In May 2025, days after Howard Lutnick announced the transfer of his ownership stake in Cantor to his children via trusts, Semafor reported that bankers at the firm had informally advised executives at Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co., which makes Nvidia's most advanced chips, on navigating Washington. Cantor called Semafor's report "baseless and false."

TSMC received billions of dollars in federal subsidies created during the Biden administration and administered by the Commerce Department to fund its U.S. manufacturing expansion.

Democratic senators called for an ethics investigation in December into whether the Lutnick family stood to benefit from Howard Lutnick's push to expand AI data centers.

And on Wednesday, Sens. Warren and Chris Coons, D-Del., demanded that Howard Lutnick, Rubio and White House chief of staff Stephen Miller preserve records on Nvidia's chip exports to China and intelligence warnings about Huang.

The senators questioned whether "prudent defensive counter-intelligence action was taken or national security recommendations were overruled."

"The place where I think the Trump administration has most failed here is in being persuaded by Jensen Huang and others to license the export of some advanced chips," Coons said Friday on CNBC's "Squawk Box," accusing the administration of turning a blind eye to the black market for more advanced chips.

#2850. SpaceX catches upper stage of Starship rocket in historic first

After a booster explosion on Friday, SpaceX successfully caught the upper stage, or spacecraft, portion of its massive Starship rocket from orbit on Saturday — a triumphant milestone for the company.

SpaceX made the historic catch using its "chopsticks," mechanical arms attached to a launch tower, at its facility in Starbase, Texas.

Test Flight 15, the most ambitious Starship test yet, marked the first time Elon Musk's company attempted to catch both the rocket's booster and upper stage.

Musk posted a video on X inspecting the upper stage craft after completing the capture.

During the first phase of the test flight on Friday, SpaceX failed to catch and recover the booster, instead diverting it to an offshore splashdown in the Gulf of Mexico where it exploded before hitting the water.

The rocket's upper stage then continued into orbit and flew around the Earth 14 times before successfully landing on SpaceX's launch tower, nicknamed Mechazilla.

The Hawaii Department of Transportation issued a temporary pause on some West Coast flights Friday, due to debris risk from the flight. Separately, the FAA issued a rare, nationwide ground stop due to telecommunications system failures. (Both of those orders have since been lifted.)

Making Starship a reliable and fully reusable rocket is critical to SpaceX's ambitions. In the near term, the company needs Starship to send more satellites into orbit to expand its Starlink satellite internet service, which is currently the company's profit center.

SpaceX is also expected to use Starship to bring astronauts and cargo from lunar orbit to the Moon's surface and back for NASA. Musk also has a grand vision to use Starship to "colonize Mars" and "make humanity multi-planetary."

During Test Flight 15 on Friday, SpaceX released another 26 of its newest Starlink v3 satellites into orbit, growing its commercial satellite internet network.

The company's constellation already included over 11,000 satellites in low Earth orbit. One Starship rocket can carry up to 60 Starlink v3 satellites, the company has said.

#2849. Knicks refind clutch gene to hold on for tight overtime win over Raptors

TORONTO — At least for one night in Canada, the clutch Knicks were back.

Jalen Brunson saved his best shot for last, Karl-Anthony Towns stuffed the boxscore on his 31st birthday, Landry Shamet shined off the bench and OG Anunoby sealed a win at the foul line as the Knicks reaffirmed their long-standing dominance over the Raptors with a 112-108 overtime victory Sunday.

Brunson's 3-pointer from the corner gave the Knicks the lead for good with 33 seconds left in overtime, a shot that allowed the point guard redemption after he botched the final possession of regulation with a slip into a turnover.

Jalen Brunson of the New York Knicks celebrates during a game against the Toronto Raptors in Toronto on Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

Karl-Anthony Towns of the New York Knicks drives to the basket against Scottie Barnes of the Toronto Raptors. NBAE via Getty Images

In OT, Brunson received an inbounds pass from Mikal Bridges — who was stellar defensively down the stretch — and knocked down the pullup jumper over Scottie Barnes. It was Brunson's biggest shot of the young season.

"I work on that shot," Brunson said, "and I'm happy it went in."

Anunoby hit a pair of clutch free throws, then thwarted a potential tying trey from Barnes, completing a comeback from 13 points down in the fourth quarter. Before Sunday, the Knicks were among the NBA's worst clutch teams, going 1-6 in games that were within one possession at any point in the final five minutes.

"That feels good. That was our time of the season where it's like, 'Let's buckle down. Let's go and work our way back for this one,' " said Shamet, who finished with 23 points in 30 minutes. "We had a few early in the year last year and obviously in the playoffs, but to get one under the belt and win one that way — that's an encouraging win for the group where maybe things didn't go the way you wanted to at times."

The Knicks (7-6) have won a remarkable 14 straight against the Raptors, a streak dating to 2023. Brunson has never lost to the division foe since joining the Knicks, but none of his victories last season were as dicey as this one.

"We know what we are capable of in those situations," Brunson said. "I'm just happy the way we fought. We could've easily given up, chalked it up to the next game. But we kept fighting."

The Knicks play the Raptors again Tuesday, giving the Knicks four days here. They left Sunday feeling good about themselves — even if they weren't satisfied with their play for much of the game.

Landry Shamet (44) of the New York Knicks shoots as Allen Graves (22) of the Toronto Raptors defends in Toronto, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

"It's always good to win close ballgames like this. Especially when you feel like you played particularly well. You give Toronto a lot of credit because they were physical and tried to push the pace and got their hands on the ball a couple times. So for us to be able to find a way to get a win in a very ugly game was huge. … It helps with that belief. And that's a huge component of what our standard and what our culture is about."

The Raptors (5-8) were playing without oft-injured star Kawhi Leonard, who is out indefinitely with knee soreness.

Towns, who turned 31 on Sunday, extended his double-double streak with 28 points and 17 boards. Brunson dropped 28 points on 9-for-22 shooting in a season-high 41 minutes.

Anunoby (44 minutes) and Towns (43) also played longer than anticipated. But sometimes it's worth it to win a close game.

#2845. How Rockstar's 'GTA VI' release could reshape gaming, streaming and even paid time off

It's been 13 years since the last installment of Grand Theft Auto, and fans are getting ready for what analysts say could be the biggest video game launch of all time.

The latest game in the franchise, "Grand Theft Auto VI," is set to launch on Nov. 19, after Take-Two Interactive's Rockstar Games delayed its launch twice. Roth analyst Eric Handler estimated the game maker spent more than $1 billion developing the game.

Sales figures could end up breaking records. Sensor Tower estimated over 12 million preorders through Nov. 13, and Morgan Stanley forecast the game could sell 40 million copies by March 2027.

Beyond launch week, the Grand Theft Auto, or GTA, franchise has a track record of lasting engagement over many years. Even 13 years after launch, "Grand Theft Auto V" is one of the top five most viewed games on Twitch this year.

In anticipation of the game's launch, investors have been taking note: Take-two's stock has more than doubled since the beginning of 2023.

The release is seen as not only a monumental moment for developer Rockstar Games and parent company Take-Two, but also a massive opportunity for the entire gaming industry.

Take-Two CEO Strauss Zelnick addressed demand in a recent "Squawk Box" interview, saying, "We tend to be pretty judicious when we talk about this sort of thing, but our expectations were high and they are being vastly exceeded so far."

A creator who goes by the name Deansocool, who has 375,000 followers on Twitch and posts content to YouTube and other social platforms as well, said "GTA VI" will enable creators to grow their audience and provide a massive opportunity for newer streamers to make a name for themselves.

"This game is going to make a lot of millionaires in 2027. A lot of creators gotta understand this is it. This is that golden ticket. This is like a hundred times bigger than Fortnite," he said in an interview with CNBC.

"It goes beyond this launch moment into this sustaining opportunity for them to grow, build, engage and retain their audience and ultimately support themselves by making money," said Twitch Chief Product Officer Mike Minton.

The release of the latest Grand Theft Auto title could draw so much attention that its impact will be felt beyond just the world of gaming.

BambooHR, an HR software platform where workers manage time-off, reported that paid time off requests for the "GTA VI" release date are nearly 13% higher than equivalent days in 2024 and 2025. It also noted that nearly one in five workers are planning to take time off for the launch.

PwC issued a report examining all the different sectors of the economy that could benefit from the game's launch — or be hurt by it. The firm projected that food delivery companies could see a surge from hunkered-down gamers, and Miami tourism could benefit. PwC noted that foreign visitors to Seoul's Bukchon Hanok Village, which inspired a location in the hit Netflix movie KPop Demon Hunters, increased 118% in the four weeks following the movie's debut.

Music streaming also looks set to get a boost. Tom Petty's "Love Is a Long Road," which appeared in the first "GTA VI" trailer in December 2023, reportedly saw Spotify streams jump by more than 37,000%.

On the downside, the game could distract from movies and streaming entertainment.

"How are people spending Thanksgiving? There's a lot of sports on TV, but you've also got a pretty solid group of your demographics that are going to be focused on playing 'GTA VI,'" said Bart Spiegel, who leads PwC's Global Entertainment & Media sector. "You're going to start to pull eyeballs away from video streaming platforms.

"I think you're going to hear a lot of analyst calls in the first quarter of 2027 saying, 'Hey, listen, some of our numbers were down because of the release of this game,'" Spiegel added.

Rockstar Games declined CNBC's interview request. Watch the video above to learn more.

#2844. Raptors fall to defending champs in overtime

The Toronto Raptors hosted the New York Knicks in the first of a home-and-home series tonight, a hard-fought, at times frustrating, and eventual narrow loss to the tune of 108-112. While this is their fourth straight loss, the seventh to a team over with.a better record, and another game where they gave up a double-digit lead, this wasn't a complete loss. Instead, it was an opportunity to see just how well the Raptors can play, making the defending champions sweat a little.

Scottie Barnes had a great first game back, barely missing a step as he found his shot from all over the floor, leading the team with 30 points, 9 boards, and 4 assists. Jamal Shead was undoubtedly Toronto's biggest difference maker though, with his intensity, speed, and facilitation shifting the tone of the game entirely in Toronto's favour every time he touched the floor. He contributed 19 points, 2 rebounds, and 4 assists. RJ, IQ, and Poeltl all chipped in double digit points as well.

Jalen Brunson played phenomenally for the Knicks, finishing with 28 points and 9 assists. OG Anunoby and Karl Anthony Towns contributed to the game as well to the tune of 21 points and 9 rebounds and 28 points and 17 rebounds, respectively. Landrey Shamet proved to be a solid bench option for New York, connecting well from long range and scoring 23 points including four 3s.

New York is still trying to find their way this year despite carrying over much of their championship roster from last year. Coach Mike Brown doesn't seem fazed in the slightest though, saying he's "happy where we are right now", recognizing the recent growing pains will better prepare them for the future. If they identify and deal with these problems now, they won't crop up as the playoffs. Especially important when you're defending a title.

Of course, for Knicks fans, some of the issues feel worth worrying about. The trifecta of their biggest concerns were things Toronto, at times, tried to capitalize on. Going into this game, New York is the slowest team in the League based on pace, with a far worse transition game than last year. Being able to get back and set up defensively is a huge asset to limit easy possessions, which actually leads to the Knicks' second problem. They are one of the worst shooting teams in the league right now, forcing contested shots in the paint rather than regrouping and making smart plays. This leads them to being the third-most blocked team in the NBA currently. Protecting the paint is crucial, Scottie and CMB the most capable of doing so. Finally, despite having some great individual defenders, collectively as a team they've underperformed, ranking 22nd and 27th in steals and blocks respectively. Taking advantage of their lacklustre defence through ball movement and finding the best shot should make the offence easy.

Scottie "our best player" Barnes was back in the lineup, a welcome sight for everyone. He took absolutely no time announcing his present, rolling off a screen and soaring in to dunk over defenders in the first possession of the game. He had 10 of their first 13 points, making a pair of long range shots in the process. An excellent indicator to his readiness to be back.

Collin Murray-Boyles got the start ahead of Poeltl, in an effort to match up better against Karl Anthony Towns who plays more as a stretch than a traditional centre. He did well, as KAT's early scoring was the result of switches that freed him from CMB.

Toronto certainly capitalized on those weaknesses early, allowing them to stay in the game despite their own turnovers, defensive lapses, and shooting struggles throughout the first quarter. They ended the frame 7-22 from the floor, but remained only two possessions behind the Knicks, 17-23.

Despite a low-scoring first quarter, there was no shortage of bodies flying to secure extra possessions when the ball was loose. Unsurprisingly, this started to translate into chippiness. Scottie Barnes almost dropped Tyler Kolek by ripping the ball from his hands. Alijah Martin thrived in this atmosphere, and was able to draw a flagrant foul from Jordan Clarkson likely from his peskiness.

The Knicks started to take their game outside the three-point line, finding a lot more success than inside the paint. New York gradually started to build a lead, but Toronto countered well, working to run them off the line and forced them to make unfavourable shots. At the same time, their own shots were starting to fall, allowing them to work their way back and eventually tie the game. Jamal Shead made his presence known with a pair of fast-break full-speed layups.

The Raptors continued to probe the paint and found more success than from beyond the arc but New York fought harder in the final minutes of the half, heading to the locker room with a narrow 47-43 lead.

The third quarter wasn't smooth sailing by any means. Unforced turnovers and miscommunications on the offensive cost the Raptors possessions. Toronto's defense kept them in the game through these slumps, forcing the Knicks to take tough shots that weren't falling for them. CMB was in foul trouble before the halfway point of the third quarter, forcing him to head to the bench, but in his absence, Poeltl managed to play solid defence in the middle.

The biggest shift came with the presence of Shead on the floor. He continued to push the pace in his minutes, forcing New York on their heels. Without a set defence, Shead was able to dictate the game, distributing to the open man but also making his own shots. In a departure to his last few games, where he was left open on the 3-point line, struggling to make shots, he feasted from long range, cashing in back-to-back buckets and forcing the Knicks to respect him. This energy gave Toronto their first significant advantage since the first frame, closing the third with a 74-67 lead.

Toronto showed flashes of disorganization again in the final frame, still working on their ability to finish games. New York weren't willing to yield and the Raptors weren't trying hard enough on every possession. Give-away rebounds that allowed the Knicks to get second and third chances were their undoing to an extent, leaving the door open for New York to get back into a game that they previously trailed by double-digits. A series of empty trips seemingly sucked all of the air out of the building until Scottie Barnes took over. The Raptors lead was back, going shot for shot with the Knicks in the final seconds, cashing in a game-tying three-pointer. Then he put his body on the line, tossing himself on the floor to trap the ball and prevent the Knicks from getting a final shot up, forcing OT.

Extra time matched the tone of the first 48 minutes of the game, with fans on their feet from start to finish. Scottie led offensively, CMB had a phenomenal block that sent the ball deep into the stands, and they were all tied up.

Inhuman shot-making by Brunson meant that the Raptors were once again behind. Clutch free-throws from Walter allowed Toronto to try and foul to regain possession, but they weren't able to make the final shot, submitting to the Knicks in overtime.

After the game, Darko attributed the loss to poor decision-making at points throughout the game, and especially in the final minutes. Shead echoed this, pointing to missed rebounds and lapses in judgement, saying "we played 44 minutes of good basketball", remarking that this is a growing experience for them, and all it will do is make them better.

They'll have their chance to make up for tonight's loss and finally break their losing streak versus the Knicks on Tuesday when they host them for a second time. Tune in at 7:30pm ET on Sportsnet.

#2843. 49ers stun Cowboys, Chiefs and Bengals impress - NFL week 10

The NFL's brightest stars lit up week 10 of the 2026 season with some astonishing plays on Sunday.

The likes of Brock Purdy, Christian McCaffrey, Patrick Mahomes, Jahmyr Gibbs and Ja'Marr Chase showcased their skills with some explosive action to help their teams to victory.

The San Francisco 49ers produced a historic display in Dallas while there were also big wins for the Kansas City Chiefs, Detroit Lions and Cincinnati Bengals.

It means the 49ers and Buffalo Bills continue to share the league's best record (8-1), while five teams moved to 7-2, including the in-form Jacksonville Jaguars.

Christian McCaffrey scored his final touchdown just as the San Francisco 49ers' general manager John Lynch made his way to the sideline in the fourth quarter [Getty Images]

The San Francisco 49ers and Dallas Cowboys have a fierce rivalry stretching back to the 1990s and they delivered another classic with a shootout in Texas.

Brock Purdy showed why he is among the leading contenders for the Most Valuable Player award as the 49ers won 46-40, racking up 584 yards of offence - the most Dallas have ever allowed at home.

San Francisco had three touchdowns of more than 65 yards, while Purdy threw for a career-high 389 yards and three touchdowns. The quarterback even blocked for one of the 49ers' other three scores, a 70-yard run by Deebo Samuel on a reverse trick play.

The highlights were two Purdy passes to Christian McCaffrey. The first saw Purdy show remarkable poise as he scrambled out of the pocket before finding McCaffrey for a 74-yard score, while the second was a perfectly-executed play over the middle that saw McCaffrey sprint in untouched from 66 yards.

Dual-threat running back McCaffrey finished with three scores and 223 total yards, including a career-high 161 receiving yards.

Purdy's MVP rival Josh Allen had a relatively quiet game as the Buffalo Bills won 31-12 at the New York Jets, while the Jacksonville Jaguars won 40-23 at the Tennessee Titans.

Including the end of last season, the Jaguars have won 15 of their past 17 regular-season games and must be considered Super Bowl contenders, especially after bringing in three key defensive players before the trade deadline on Tuesday.

Joe Burrow became the new MVP favourite last week and continued his superb form as the Cincinnati Bengals romped to a 38-18 win in their divisional match-up against the Pittsburgh Steelers.

New Manchester United boss Mauricio Pochettino was among the crowd as Burrow was involved in four touchdowns for the fifth straight game, tying an NFL record.

The Bengals quarterback had 300 passing yards before half-time and finished with 409. Most of them were to Ja'Marr Chase, who claimed 231 yards from just 11 catches for the 7-2 Bengals.

Their divisional rivals, the Cleveland Browns, pulled off another comeback as they recovered from 17-0 down to beat the Houston Texans 27-24.

The Browns have won their opening four home games for the first time since 1963 and are now 6-4.

Quarterback Deshaun Watson led the Browns to a game-winning drive in the fourth quarter of all six of those wins and he said this one was "definitely special" as it came against his former team.

Amon-Ra St Brown met Bayern Munich forward Jamal Musiala at the Allianz Arena and waved the German flag before and after Sunday's game [Getty Images]

Munich staged the penultimate international game of the season and just playing in Germany was a "dream come true" for the Detroit Lions' wide receiver Amon-Ra St Brown.

The 27-year-old's mother is German, and he and his two brothers speak the language fluently and have been regular visitors to their mother's homeland.

St Brown, who arrived wearing lederhosen and waved the German flag as he ran on to the field, scored two touchdowns as the Lions charged to a 35-10 win over the New England Patriots.

Yet St Brown was outshone by team-mate Jahmyr Gibbs, who had 99 receiving yards, 153 rushing yards and two touchdowns, taking him to 62 overall - a new NFL record in a player's first four seasons.

The Patriots lost Drake Maye to an ankle injury when he was sacked on the game's opening drive by Aidan Hutchinson, who injured his knee as he celebrated, and the Lions fear it is a significant injury.

Detroit, the Minnesota Vikings and Chicago Bears are now all 7-2 in the ultra-competitive NFC North, with the Vikings bouncing back after suffering back-to-back defeats.

Kirk Cousins returned to Minnesota before the trade deadline on Tuesday and the veteran quarterback led them to a 30-10 win at their other divisional rival, the 6-4 Green Bay Packers, with their defence claiming eight sacks - the most in a single game this season.

The Las Vegas Raiders allowed Cousins to rejoin Minnesota as they felt Fernando Mendoza, this year's first overall draft pick, was ready to become their starting quarterback.

Mendoza's first start came against the Seattle Seahawks, the Super Bowl champions, and he managed to run in a 10-yard touchdown on the Raiders' opening drive.

But the Raiders' offence then struggled and Mendoza was sacked six times as the Seahawks won 21-13.

"It's good to get punched in the face because now it's a great opportunity to grow and learn," said Mendoza.

Seattle quarterback Drew Lock made two big touchdown passes on the run, one to Cooper Kupp and the other a 71-yard score by Jaxon Smith-Njigba.

How mum's struggle inspired Mendoza's rise to top NFL draft pick

The Kansas City Chiefs' run game has caught the eye so far this season, in particular new signing Kenneth Walker, but he was out injured on Sunday as Patrick Mahomes reminded everyone he has one of the best arms in the business.

For the first time since 2022, the three-time Super Bowl winner threw for five touchdowns, plus 348 yards, as Kansas City won 35-13 at the Atlanta Falcons.

Running back Emmett Johnson stepped up in the absence of Walker to claim 101 rushing yards, 70 receiving yards and two touchdowns.

Mahomes made explosive plays to Cyrus Allen and Andrew Armstrong for their first NFL scores, while fellow wide receiver Josh Downs scored on his debut after the Chiefs traded for him on deadline day.

That took Mahomes to 332 career touchdown passes, including the play-offs, putting the 31-year-old top of the all-time list for the most in a player's first 10 seasons.

The Chiefs are 7-2 and will need their receiving corps to ease the burden on Walker during a tough five-game stretch after next week.

Smyth scores 55-yard field goal in Saints defeat

#2842. Knicks 112, Raptors 108 (OT): Scenes from our first bonus basketball of the season

There's trouble when Mike Breen calls the Knicks out. Right before tip-off, the lifelong fan and broadcasting GOAT called them a "mediocre" basketball team, then acknowledged that they know it's a problem and they're trying to fix it. We noticed it, too; New York had lost two straight and been outhustled by the outclassed Indiana Pacers on Friday.

For 40 minutes tonight, the Knicks proved Mike's case. They led after one because Toronto couldn't shoot, and much the same was true at halftime. Then the Dinos began to pull away, nearly doubling up the Knicks in the paint and beating them to the boards. The Americans trailed by seven to start the fourth and by as many as 14 before finally showing some fire and desire. They went ahead with less than a minute left, but Scottie Barnes (30 PTS, 9 RBS, -9) scored Toronto's last nine points to force overtime. The Knicks never trailed in the fifth frame, and a Brunson dagger with 33 seconds left put them ahead for good, 112-108.

Back from a groin injury, Barnes scored 10 of Toronto's first 13 points, hitting back-to-back threes to put the Raptors up 13-4. Scottie was merriment and light while the Knicks were a smear of discouragement. Birthday boy Karl-Anthony Towns (28 PTS, 17 RBS) pitched two passes into the front row in the first four minutes, and Jalen Brunson (28 PTS, 9 AST, 41 MIN) had three dimes and one basket in the quarter. The bench improved matters. A Mikal Bridges (5 PTS, 7 RBS, 32 MIN . . . +15!) steal and a Towns bunny sparked a 9-0 run that included five points from Landry Shamet (23 PTS, 30 MIN, +12) and a tying dunk from Andre Drummond, courtesy of Josh Hart (0 PTS, 5 RBS, 26 MIN, -15).

Allen Graves stripped Brunson and Jakob Poeltl laid it in at the other end to put Toronto up 17-14. Then the Dinos, hardly renowned for their shooting, came up blank for the final 3:45. Tyler Kolek fed Towns for the tying three and Miles McBride for the one that made it 23-17 at the break.

Shamet scored six quick points to put the Knicks up 32-22. He hit a three after Kolek picked off an RJ Barrett pass, then made all three free throws after Barrett fouled him on another attempt. Toronto answered with a 14-2 run. Poeltl picked Jordan Clarkson's pocket and finished at the other end, starting center Collin Murray-Boyles dunked back a shot Shamet had blocked, and Jamal Shead (19 PTS) scored eight points in 76 seconds to put the Raptors ahead 38-36. During Toronto's 10-0 stretch, the Knicks burned a full shot clock and missed four straight shots.

OG Anunoby stopped the run with two free throws, and Towns put New York back in front with a three. Barrett tied it at 42, but Brunson answered with a step-back three, his only field goal of the quarter. Bridges, who'd barely figured into the offense, set up that shot with 45 seconds left and hit his first basket, a 21-footer, with seven seconds to go. Barnes managed just two points in the second, and Barrett had as many turnovers as baskets (two).

The Knicks were lucky to lead 47-43 at the half. The shooting gap was small (44% to 41%), but Toronto owned the paint, 26-14, won the boards by four, and turned 12 Knicks giveaways into 16 points. The three-ball kept our heroes afloat (42% to Toronto's 26%). For those keeping track, Bridges was 1-for-2 for two points, with two rebounds, an assist, and a steal . . . but stay tuned for this:

On to Q3: the Knicks led 57-54 midway through the third before Toronto scored seven straight. Brunson's three cut it to 63-62, but the Raptors answered with an 11-0 run, including two Shead threes (the kid was cooking). Barrett had eight points and three assists, and Shead scored nine as Toronto won the quarter 31-20 and took a 74-67 lead into the fourth. Cap scored 13 of New York's 20 points, KAT had four, Bridges made one free throw, and Hart continued to deal with a devastating allergy to buckets.

On to Q4: Immanuel Quickley hit two threes from at least 29 feet to put Toronto up 84-71 with 8:55 left. The Knicks answered with a 12-2 run. Kolek found Anunoby for two quick buckets, and Towns picked Barrett's pocket to send Bridges the other way for a layup. Towns had 15 points and seven rebounds in the quarter, while Brunson went scoreless but handed out five assists.

Down five with under two minutes left, New York scored seven straight. Bridges grabbed the offensive board that set up Shamet's tying three, and Towns put the Knicks ahead with a hook. Towns then hit from 28 feet to make it 95-92 with 20 seconds left, but Barnes answered with a step-back three six seconds later. He had 11 in the quarter and put the clamps on Brunson as time expired.

On to OT: Shamet and Brunson scored seven apiece in the bonus frame. Quickley kept Toronto close with two early buckets, one off a Bridges turnover. Barnes whiffed on free throws with 1:47 left, banked in a three to tie it at 104, and lobbed to Murray-Boyles to tie it again at 106. Out of a timeout, Bridges found Brunson for the go-ahead fadeaway three with 33 seconds left. Anunoby hit both free throws to restore the three-point cushion, Barnes missed a chance to tie it, Towns grabbed his 17th rebound, and then he split a pair at the line to seal the win.

Mo is gassing up his recap. The Knicks stay over the border for game with the Raps on Tuesday. Rest up, Knickerbockers.

#2841. $180 million Van Gogh tests the art market and collector confidence

A version of this article appeared in CNBC's Inside Wealth newsletter, a weekly guide to the high-net-worth investor and consumer. Sign up to receive future editions straight to your inbox.

The art auctions in New York next week could top $2 billion, as a treasure trove of masterpieces comes to the market for the first time in decades.

Total sales at Sotheby's, Christie's and Phillips are expected to be around $2.4 billion, which would equal last year's total, according to ArtTactic. Sotheby's alone is expected to pull in over $1.1 billion, with a single collection from The Blaquier-Arrieta Collection estimated at $450 million.

The collection includes the two big headliners of the week: Vincent van Gogh's landscape "Châtaigniers en fleurs," (Chestnut Trees in Flower) estimated at $180 million and Paul Cézanne's "Arlequin," estimated at $120 million. Neither has been seen in public for a half a century.

Sotheby's CEO Charles Stewart said demand is strong in the art market, especially for the highest quality blue-chip works, as rising stocks markets and private wealth boost the confidence of collectors.

"There's been enormous wealth creation and a lot of confidence in markets," Stewart said. "It's a global phenomenon. We're seeing recovery and greater interest across Asia and something really exciting happening from the Middle East across to Singapore and Europe and the U.S."

Christie's star of the week is Wassily Kandinsky's "Betonte Ecken," estimated at $60 million to $80 million. Christie's CEO Bonnie Brennan describes the market is "confident but discerning, rewarding truly exceptional works." She said recent gyrations in the stock market and fears of an AI bubble aren't likely to deter serious collectors.

"At the very highest end of our market, collectors tend to take a much longer-term view," Brennan said. "They understand that the opportunity to acquire a masterpiece may come only once in a generation, and that can be far more important than what the stock market has done in any particular week. In uncertain moments, the very best can actually become even more compelling."

The art market has continued to rebound this year after declines in 2023 and 2024, with auction sales in the first half up 70% over last year, to $6.8 billion, according to Bank of America.

Drew Watson, head of art services at Bank of America, said blue-chip art is outperforming, with Impressionist & Modern sales up 133% in the first half.

"Our outlook for the art market this November is cautiously optimistic," Watson said. "Improving liquidity, combined with an unusually strong concentration of fresh-to-market private collections, creates a favorable backdrop for the marquee sales."

The Inside Wealth newsletter by Robert Frank is your weekly guide to high-net-worth investors and the industries that serve them.

#2840. Motta: "È sempre stato il mio sogno da piccolino cercare di fare diventare la mia passione un lavoro..."

"La motivazione bisogna trovarla dentro di sé perchè è il lavoro più bello del mondo..."

Edoardo Motta è intervenuto post amichevole ai microfoni di Lazio Style.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

È stata una bella partita, una sgambata, allenamento congiunto. È andata bene.

Quando il portiere gioca poco è difficile rivedere il campo, la porta sembra sempre più grossa e il campo più grande. Però sono contento anche dalla mia prestazione.

Certo è una cosa che aiuta tutti, stimolante per tutto il gruppo.

Il mister, i preparatori dei portieri ci stimolano giorno dopo giorno. La motivazione bisogna trovarla dentro di sé perchè è il lavoro più bello del mondo. È sempre stato il mio sogno da piccolino cercare di fare diventare la mia passione un lavoro.

L'anno scorso sono contento di come è andata, non mi aspettavo di arrivare in Serie A. Con Gattuso il portiere è un giocatore attivo, è un qualcosa che aiuta la squadra ed è la prima cosa fondamentale. È una cosa in più, un bagaglio in più che si può portare dietro.

Penso sia sotto gli occhi di tutti. I risultati di adesso sono visibili a tutti.

Miglior difesa del campionato? È un dato che non avevo letto, può essere il sistema di gioco e non lo prenderei come dato di riferimento.

#2839. NFL draft order: Chargers-Ravens could reshape first round

Sunday night's game between the Cincinnati Bengals and the Pittsburgh Steelers was filled with playoff implications. Both teams entered the game with matching 6-2 records, and a spot atop the division was at stake.

Monday night's contest between the 0-8 Los Angeles Chargers, and the 4-5 Baltimore Ravens, does not have the same playoff weight. But it could go a long way towards deciding what team has the first pick in the 2027 NFL Draft.

#2838. Player Grades: Lakers vs. Suns

As it turns out, the only time the Lakers were in trouble on Sunday was when the Suns finally benched Brooks, who stuffed the stat sheet with one point, two rebounds, three fouls and four turnovers in 22 minutes while tallying a plus-minus of -20.

So, let's dive into the win. As always, grades are based on expectations for each player. A "B" grade represents the average performance for that player.

26 minutes, 12 points, 8 rebounds, 3 steals, 2 blocks, 3 fouls, 4 turnovers, 4-9 FG, 2-6 3PT, 2-2 FT, +30

One of Thybulle's better games this season. Eight rebounds are a nice bonus to his typical production, which also showed up with his five stocks. He also had an absurd plus-minus of +30 in 26 minutes.

32 minutes, 12 points, 3 rebounds, 3 assists, 1 steal, 3 blocks, 2 fouls, 4-10 FG, 2-6 3PT, 2-2 FT, +13

Jake struggled to shoot the ball, but still filled the stat sheet. An additional four stocks from LaRavia shows how impressive this starting lineup was defensively.

32 minutes, 14 points, 12 rebounds, 3 assists, 1 steal, 2 blocks, 2 fouls, 2 turnovers, 4-5 FG, 6-6 FT, +18

Kessler was an absolute monster on the offensive glass with half of his rebounds coming on that end. Against the one team worse at rebounding than the Lakers, Kessler ensured they exploited that weakness.

37 minutes, 22 points, 6 rebounds, 1 assist, 2 steals, 3 fouls, 3 turnovers, 7-10 FG, 5-6 3PT, 3-4 FT, +21

In the small sample size of this season, Grimes as a starter has been dramatically better than Grimes as a reserve. Is there more to that? Should he move into the starting lineup? It's hard to argue taking either Thybulle or LaRavia out right now.

35 minutes, 43 points, 3 rebounds, 9 assists, 1 steal, 5 fouls, 2 turnovers, 13-28 FG, 5-13 3PT, 12-14 FT, +10

Luka continued his impressive first-quarter scoring and got the Lakers out to a great start with nearly half his points coming in the opening 12 minutes. His shot selection in the fourth when he returned was pretty questionable but he got to the rim when he needed to late in the game.

18 minutes, 6 points, 2 rebounds, 3 assists, 2 steals, 4 fouls, 1 turnover, 2-3 FG, 0-1 3PT, 2-2 FT, -18

A quieter night from Ziaire, though it still featured an impressive reverse alley-oop.

#2837. Bessent calls for ‘dramatic shakeup’ at Nvidia, urges outside CEO

Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent on Sunday called for a "dramatic shakeup" at Nvidia after CEO Jensen Huang's resignation, questioning whether the chipmaker pursued illicit sales of its advanced artificial intelligence chips to boost earnings.

Bessent said Nvidia's board should look outside the company for its next chief executive and suggested other executives should face scrutiny over the chip giant's compliance with U.S. export controls.

"I want to keep coming back to what was the culture at Nvidia, and were they pressing these seemingly illicit sales to goose their revenues and their earnings?" Bessent said in an interview on Fox News.

The remarks come amid a growing feud between Bessent and Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick over the suspected diversion of advanced AI chips to China, even as the Treasury secretary continues to defend President Donald Trump's handling of the matter.

Both Bessent and Lutnick are expected to accompany Trump to China this week, Semafor reported Sunday, putting the two Cabinet secretaries alongside the president as he prepares to meet with Chinese President Xi Jinping and Russian President Vladimir Putin on the sidelines of the APEC summit.

A White House official told Reuters on Thursday that the president maintains "complete confidence" in Lutnick, despite mounting scrutiny of the secretary’s handling of the Nvidia matter.

Huang resigned Thursday following revelations about his relationship with Alice Huang, a former executive at Singapore-based Megaspeed, whom U.S. intelligence officials suspect of spying for China. Megaspeed is suspected of diverting Nvidia chips to China in violation of U.S. export controls.

Nvidia named Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress interim CEO.

Bessent suggested Sunday that Nvidia's problems extended well beyond Huang.

"If this were a bank and they hadn't complied like this, the OCC would be in there right now, and a lot of the executives would be removed," Bessent said, referring to the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency.

Bessent also questioned Nvidia's financial disclosures, pointing to a surge in reported Singapore revenue as its China sales fell.

Nvidia stopped separately reporting Singapore revenue in November 2025, saying a new methodology based on customers' headquarters rather than billing addresses better reflected the geographic distribution of its sales.

Bessent's remarks came two days after he told CNBC's "Squawk Box" that several federal agencies had supported adding Megaspeed to the government's export blacklist for months.

"But Commerce has the pen," Bessent said.

The public rebuke followed reporting by Axios that Bessent confronted Lutnick in the White House Situation Room over Commerce's failure to act against Megaspeed. Commerce denied that the confrontation took place.

Lutnick is also facing scrutiny over his family's financial ties to Nvidia. CNBC reported Friday that Cantor Fitzgerald, the Wall Street firm formerly run by Lutnick and now led by his sons, has helped arrange billions of dollars in financing for Nvidia's business partners.

Cantor said Nvidia share holdings fell nearly 50% over six months as national security concerns surrounding Huang escalated. The positions were associated with client swap transactions, not proprietary investments, the investment bank said.

There is no evidence Lutnick shared sensitive information with his sons or that Cantor traded on such information.

Bessent on Sunday said Trump had drawn a firm line against sales of chips more advanced than Nvidia's H200 to China. Trump himself authorized H200 sales to approved Chinese customers in December —following months of lobbying by Huang — in an arrangement under which the U.S. government would receive a 25% cut.

Bessent's latest criticism comes two days before Nvidia is scheduled to report its first quarterly earnings since Huang's departure.

#2835. Megaspeed says Alice Huang left in 2025 as it launches internal probe into reports about its business

Megaspeed told CNBC Sunday that Alice Huang left the company in August 2025 and said it has launched an internal probe into media reports concerning its business and supply chain.

The Singapore-based cloud computing company has faced heightened scrutiny since former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang said he had been unfaithful to his wife following reports linking him to former Megaspeed executive Alice Huang.

U.S. intelligence officials believe Alice Huang is a Chinese intelligence asset, according to The New York Times.

Jensen Huang resigned as CEO of Nvidia on Nov. 12 amid fallout from reports about his extramarital relationships, one of which was reportedly a direct report.

"Ms. Alice Huang officially left Megaspeed effective August 30, 2025 and currently has no role with the Company," Megaspeed said in an emailed statement shared with CNBC. "Megaspeed does not comment on Ms. Huang's personal and private matters," it added.

Megaspeed describes itself as a "core business partner" of Nvidia's computing power cloud business in the region, allowing customers to rent computing power from Nvidia GPUs, or graphics processing units — specialized chips designed to do many calculations at the same time.

The company, which has offices in Singapore and Indonesia, has previously faced media and government scrutiny over whether chips supplied through its business were redirected to customers in China.

Megaspeed said Sunday that media reports about its business, supply chain and global operations are not supported by verifiable evidence and it has retained external legal counsel to conduct a "thorough internal investigation."

"Megaspeed designs and maintains its global operational and supply-chain control frameworks to comply with all applicable laws and regulatory regimes, including United States export control requirements and local jurisdiction rules in every market where we operate," it said.

The company added that it will review the reporting and communicate further with stakeholders as appropriate.

— CNBC's Anniek Bao and Jenni Reid helped contribute to this report.

#2834. NATO member Estonia to close Russia border after attempted 'sabotage' of drone maker

NATO member Estonia says it is preparing to close road border crossings with Russia from Dec. 1, as it blames Moscow for an attempted "sabotage" of one of its defense companies.

Estonian Foreign Minister Margus Tsahkna said on Sunday that Russia's military intelligence service GRU had ordered an arson attack against Estonian drone maker Threod Systems in the early hours of Saturday morning.

Authorities said the attempted attack did not cause significant damage and eight people were detained in connection with it.

Estonia, which shares a 294-kilometer (183-mile) border with Russia, said closing the crossings would help to reduce Moscow's ability to conduct hostile activities against it and mitigate risks related to sanctions circumvention.

Tallinn has frequently raised the alarm over sabotage attacks against NATO countries that have been supporting Ukraine since Russia launched the full-scale invasion of its neighbor in February 2022.

"This weekend's attempted sabotage against an Estonian defence company, attributed by our security authorities to Russia's GRU, is yet another reminder that Russia's hostile activities extend far beyond Ukraine," Estonian Foreign Minister Margus Tsahkna said Sunday via social media.

"Estonia is preparing to close its road border crossings with Russia in response. Hostile actions must have consequences."

Tsahkna said he had informed his Finnish, Lithuanian and Latvian counterparts of the decision.

Russia's foreign ministry said it would assess Estonia's plans to close its border crossings, adding that if these plans were to come into force, it would be seen as "yet another bout of aggressive and unbridled Russophobia," according to state news agency Tass.

It did not directly comment on the accusation that Moscow is responsible for the attack on Threod Systems.

"Estonia is building walls. Many countries, as history shows, have built walls that have brought no good to their creators," Kremlin spokesman ​Dmitry Peskov said Monday, according to comments reported by Russian state news outlet Ria Novosti.

Estonian Prime Minister Kristen Michal said the attack was part of Russia's broader "hybrid war" against Europe.

"We will not be intimidated. Estonia is preparing for the possibility of closing its border with Russia to reduce the risks to our people and national security," Michal said Sunday via social media.

The latest incident follows an arson attack in mid-August against Milrem Robotics, an Estonian defense company that supplies Ukraine with unmanned military vehicles. Estonian authorities later blamed Russia for the attack. Moscow denied the allegation.

European security officials and lawmakers have previously warned about Russia's hybrid warfare. In September, Danish intelligence officials said Russia could launch a "limited" military attack against a NATO member in the coming months, saying the Kremlin appears more willing to take risks after more than 4½ years of war against Ukraine.

The Danish Defence Intelligence Service, or DDIS, identified a "low but growing risk" that Russia could carry out isolated strikes with long-range weapons on infrastructure that is critical to the support of Ukraine.

#2833. As LeBron soars up all-time assist list, his teammates need to have their hands ready

Fourteen games into the season, the Sixers are noticing the LeBron James effect in just about every facet of life. After a win over the Indiana Pacers, Tyrese Maxey zoomed to the podium to knock out his media availability with the majority of the Sixers beat in the locker room interviewing James.

"I love LeBron James," Maxey said as he sat down in front of a room with only a couple reporters.

Of course, they're noticing it in plenty of ways on the court as well. That win saw LeBron record 11 assists, good enough to move him up ahead of Jason Kidd for third in all-time career assists. He now only trails Chris Paul and John Stockton.

It may not sound like it would be, but playing next to such a prolific passer is something they have to get used to. The fact that your teammate could hit you with the ball at any given moment is an adjustment.

On the first play of the game, Maxey was cutting down the baseline just for the sake of cutting through. He thought all he was doing was helping create space on the side of the floor he was vacating, not realizing that LeBron had found him wide open under the basket.

"I was not planning to get the ball and the ball hit me in my chest and I looked up I was at the rim so I had to lay it in and I missed it," Maxey said, "but it's just like plays like that where you got to be ready at all times."

The idea of being ready for the ball at any time when James is on the floor is something that the Sixers are trying to make a point of emphasis. As was the case with Maxey's smoked bunny, the process of learning that can look ugly at times.

James joked that the key to guys figuring it out is to "hit 'em in the head a couple of times." According to James, VJ Edgecombe was a particularly frequent victim of that during training camp.

"Even when you don't think you're open, you're gonna be open," is what James told Edgecombe and, frankly, all of his teammates. "I just tell my guys, always be paying attention to me when I got the ball because I definitely see plays develop before they happen."

Off-ball movement is often looked at as "dirty work" in the NBA, something the Sixers haven't excelled at in recent years. Because of James' ability to reward cutters, it's made the Sixers not just buy into it, but happy to do so.

"He makes you want to cut. He makes you want to play without the ball," Maxey said. "He makes you want to always have your hands ready and be ready to score the ball because he's that unselfish and he's going to find you."

They say raw assist numbers are not the truest mark of a passer or a facilitator. One of James' best assists of the game against Indiana was not given to him in the stat sheet, but it was entirely created by what he saw from outside the perimeter.

Dean Wade cut out of the strong side corner to open that up from the ball handlers, before hearing James tell him to run back. James saw that Wade's defender would help on Edgecombe once Edgecombe drove to the basket, leaving Wade wide open for a corner three. Steve Jones, a brilliant Xs and Os guy for The Dunker Spot, clipped the play.

After being around James for training camp and a handful of regular season games, Wade said he's not really surprised by LeBron's ability to make that kind of read in the moment.

"He sees everything that's going on," Wade said. "He knows what their defense is doing."

James has had a reputation for being able to diagnose opposing team's plays as they're being called. Pacers head coach Rick Carlisle, who called James an "NBA basketball AI machine," said that LeBron is the reason he doesn't have play calls anymore. James said that ability is something he's just always had with him.

"I've always, since I was a kid, had the ability to see plays before they actually happened," he said, "seeing the movement, being able to see like cracks in the defense before they was even open. It's something that's been with me since I was a kid."

The Sixers have struggled so far to put together all of this absurd offensive talent in a way that really clicks. Even after beating the Pacers, they are still 19th in the league in points per 100 possessions, per Cleaning the Glass.

Getting used to the tendencies of one of the best passers of all time should help things look smoother as the season goes on.

#2831. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

A win is not always pretty. A Sunday afternoon game against the Utah Jazz got weird quickly, but the San Antonio Spurs were able to pull out a 110-102 win – their 12th in a row.

It probably wouldn't have happened without the late-game heroics of Dylan Harper, who scored a career-high 31 points.

As usual, Harper was excellent in transition. All game long, he ran the break to score buckets through and around the Jazz. This play shows what makes Harper tough to cover in transition. He's so fast and has a big frame that blocks defenders from getting to the ball, and he finishes with soft touch at the rim, even under duress.
